 **D.** Oppose any change in the current flowing through it ✓
**Answer:** Oppose any change in the current flowing through it

**Q2745.** An ideal step-up transformer increases the:
 **A.** Power
 **B.** Current
 **C.** Frequency
 **D.** Voltage ✓
**Answer:** Voltage

**Q2746.** A straight conductor of length 0.5 m is moved with a speed of 10 m/s at an angle of 30 degrees to a magnetic field of 0.8 T. The induced EMF is:
 **A.** 4 V
 **B.** 3.46 V
 **C.** 0 V
 **D.** 2 V ✓
**Answer:** 2 V

**Q2747.** Cores of electromagnets are made of ferromagnetic materials like soft iron to provide:
 **A.** Low permeability and low retentivity
 **B.** High permeability and high retentivity
 **C.** Low permeability and high retentivity
 **D.** High permeability and low retentivity ✓
**Answer:** High permeability and low retentivity

**Q2748.** The process of generating electricity in a modern power plant most closely relies on the discoveries of:
 **A.** Isaac Newton
 **B.** Albert Einstein
 **C.** James Clerk Maxwell
 **D.** Michael Faraday ✓
**Answer:** Michael Faraday

**Q2749.** An inductor blocks AC but allows DC to pass. Why?
 **A.** It has infinite resistance for AC and zero resistance for DC
 **B.** The magnetic field of DC is stronger
 **C.** DC current has more energy than AC current
 **D.** AC involves a changing current, which induces a large opposing back EMF ✓
**Answer:** AC involves a changing current, which induces a large opposing back EMF

**Q2750.** If you double the number of turns and the length of a long solenoid while keeping the area constant, its self-inductance will be:
 **A.** Halved
 **B.** The same
 **C.** Quadrupled
 **D.** Doubled ✓
**Answer:** Doubled

**Q2751.** A small, permanent magnet is pushed into a coil and then pulled out at the same speed. The induced current will be:
 **A.** In the same direction during both motions
 **B.** Zero during both motions
 **C.** Only present when the magnet is pushed in, not when pulled out
 **D.** In opposite directions during the two motions ✓
**Answer:** In opposite directions during the two motions

---

### Electromagnetism (105 Questions)

**Q2752.** The magnetic field inside a long, current-carrying solenoid is:
**Answer:** For a long solenoid, the field lines are concentrated and nearly parallel inside, creating a uniform field.

**Q2753.** A proton enters a uniform magnetic field perpendicularly. The path of the proton will be a:
**Answer:** The magnetic force provides the necessary centripetal force for circular motion, as it is always perpendicular to the velocity.

**Q2754.** The current in a 50 mH inductor changes from 10 A to 2 A in 0.2 seconds. What is the magnitude of the average EMF induced in the inductor?
**Answer:** Using E = -L (ΔI / Δt). The magnitude is E = (50 x 10⁻³ H) \* |(2-10)A| / 0.2s = 0.05 \* (8 / 0.2) = 0.05 \* 40 = 2.0 V.

**Q2755.** Lenz's Law is a consequence of the law of conservation of:
**Answer:** The induced current opposes the change in flux, meaning work must be done against this opposition, which is converted into electrical energy.

**Q2756.** The SI unit of magnetic flux is the:
**Answer:** One Weber is equal to one Tesla-meter squared (T·m²), representing the total magnetic field lines passing through a surface.

**Q2757.** A transformer is a device that:
**Answer:** Transformers operate on the principle of mutual induction to step up or step down AC voltages.

**Q2758.** The force on a current-carrying conductor in a magnetic field is maximum when the angle between the conductor and the field is:
**Answer:** The force is given by F = ILBsin(θ). Since sin(90 degrees) = 1, the force is at its maximum value.

**Q2759.** An AC generator works on the principle of:
**Answer:** The rotation of a coil in a magnetic field changes the magnetic flux, inducing an electromotive force (EMF).

**Q2760.** The direction of the induced current in a circuit is always such as to oppose the change in magnetic flux that produces it. This is a statement of:
**Answer:** This law specifically addresses the direction of the induced current, attributing it to the conservation of energy.

**Q2761.** What is the charge-to-mass ratio (e/m) for an electron?
**Answer:** This value is determined experimentally by observing the electron's motion in electric and magnetic fields.

**Q2762.** In a step-up transformer, the secondary coil has:
**Answer:** More turns in the secondary increase voltage, stepping it up.

**Q2763.** A galvanometer can be converted into an ammeter by connecting a:
**Answer:** A low-resistance shunt connected in parallel allows most of the current to bypass the galvanometer, enabling the measurement of larger currents.

**Q2764.** Motional EMF is induced when a conductor:
**Answer:** The movement of the conductor through the magnetic field causes a change in magnetic flux, inducing an EMF.

**Q2765.** The energy stored in an inductor is given by the formula:
**Answer:** This equation correctly describes the energy stored in the magnetic field of an inductor.

**Q2766.** Magnetic flux will be maximum when the angle between the magnetic field and the area vector is:
**Answer:** Flux is given by Φ = BAcos(θ). When θ = 0 degrees, cos(0 degrees) = 1, which is the maximum value.

**Q2767.** In the equation for the magnetic force on a charge, F = qvBsin(θ), θ is the angle between:
**Answer:** The angle θ is defined as the angle between the velocity vector of the charge and the magnetic field vector.

**Q2768.** The phenomenon of producing an EMF in a coil due to a change of current in the same coil is called:
**Answer:** This process, also known as inductance, describes how a coil induces a 'back EMF' in itself to oppose a change in current.

**Q2769.** A CRO (Cathode Ray Oscilloscope) uses which of the following to deflect the electron beam?
**Answer:** Most CROs use pairs of deflection plates that create electric fields to control the beam's vertical and horizontal position.

**Q2770.** An ideal transformer has 100% efficiency. This means:
**Answer:** Efficiency is the ratio of output power to input power. 100% efficiency means no power is lost in the transformation.

**Q2771.** The torque on a current-carrying loop in a magnetic field is maximum when the plane of the loop is:
**Answer:** When the plane of the loop is parallel to the field, the forces on the sides create the largest turning effect (torque).

**Q2772.** Which of the following materials is most suitable for the core of an electromagnet?
**Answer:** Soft iron is easily magnetized and demagnetized, making it ideal for creating a strong temporary magnet.

**Q2773.** If the speed of a charged particle moving through a magnetic field is doubled, the magnetic force on it will:
**Answer:** According to the formula F = qvBsin(θ), the force is directly proportional to the velocity 'v'.

**Q2774.** In a velocity selector, a charged particle will pass through undeflected if the electric and magnetic forces cancel. If the electric field is 600 N/C and the magnetic field is 0.02 T, what is the speed of the undeflected particle?
**Answer:** For zero deflection, qE = qvB, which simplifies to v = E/B. v = (600 N/C) / (0.02 T) = 30000 m/s = 3 x 10⁴ m/s.

**Q2775.** The magnetic field outside an ideal toroid is:
**Answer:** Due to its geometry, the magnetic field lines are completely contained within the windings of an ideal toroid.

**Q2776.** According to Faraday's law, the induced EMF is directly proportional to the:
**Answer:** Faraday's Law states that E = -N (dΦ/dt), meaning the EMF is proportional to how quickly the flux changes.

**Q2777.** To convert a galvanometer into a voltmeter, one should connect a:
**Answer:** A large series resistor (a multiplier) limits the current flowing through the galvanometer, allowing the device to be connected in parallel with a component to measure a large potential difference.

**Q2778.** Two parallel wires carry current in the same direction. The force between them will be:
**Answer:** The magnetic field produced by one wire interacts with the current in the other wire, resulting in a force that pulls them together.

**Q2779.** A changing electric flux creates a:
**Answer:** This is one of the core concepts of Maxwell's equations (specifically, the Maxwell-Ampere law). A changing electric field acts as a source of a magnetic field.

**Q2780.** The unit Henry (H) can also be expressed as:
**Answer:** Since inductance L = Φ / I, its unit is Weber per Ampere.

**Q2781.** Back EMF in a motor is greatest at:
**Answer:** Back EMF is proportional to the rotational speed of the motor's armature. The faster it spins, the greater the rate of change of flux, and the larger the back EMF.

**Q2782.** The working principle of a moving coil galvanometer is that a current-carrying coil in a magnetic field experiences:
**Answer:** The forces on opposite sides of the coil create a turning couple, or torque, which causes the coil to rotate.

**Q2783.** Eddy currents are undesirable in a transformer because they:
**Answer:** These induced currents circulate within the core, and due to the core's resistance, they dissipate energy in the form of heat (I²R losses).

**Q2784.** A stationary charge in a magnetic field experiences a force of:
**Answer:** The magnetic force formula is F = qvBsin(θ). If the velocity v=0, the force is zero. Magnetic fields only exert forces on moving charges.

**Q2785.** To reduce energy loss due to eddy currents, transformer cores are:
**Answer:** The core is built from thin, insulated iron sheets. This lamination increases the overall resistance and confines the eddy currents to small loops, significantly reducing energy loss.

**Q2786.** A current of 2 A is flowing in a coil of inductance 5 H. The energy stored in the coil is:
**Answer:** Using the formula E = 1/2 LI², we get E = 1/2 \* (5) \* (2²) = 1/2 \* (5) \* (4) = 10 J.

**Q2787.** The relationship between the magnetic field B, velocity v, and motional EMF (E) in a conductor of length L is given by:
**Answer:** This is the correct formula for motional EMF when the field, velocity, and conductor are mutually perpendicular.

**Q2788.** What is the primary cause of the magnetic field of the Earth?
**Answer:** The movement of molten iron and nickel in the outer core creates a self-sustaining dynamo effect, generating the Earth's magnetic field.

**Q2789.** If the number of turns in a solenoid is doubled while keeping the length and current constant, the magnetic field inside will:
**Answer:** The formula for the magnetic field is B = μ₀nI, where n = N/L. If N is doubled, n is doubled, and B is doubled.

**Q2790.** A device that measures very small currents with high sensitivity is the:
**Answer:** A galvanometer is the core instrument designed to detect and measure minute electric currents.

**Q2791.** What is the path of an electron projected parallel to a uniform magnetic field?
**Answer:** The angle θ between velocity and the magnetic field is 0 degrees. Since sin(0 degrees)=0, the magnetic force is zero, and the particle continues undeflected.

**Q2792.** A 20 cm long wire carries a current of 10 A and is placed in a 0.4 T magnetic field. If the wire makes an angle of 30° with the field, what is the magnetic force on the wire?
**Answer:** Using F = ILBsin(θ). F = (10 A) \* (0.2 m) \* (0.4 T) \* sin(30°) = 2 \* 0.4 \* 0.5 = 0.4 N.

**Q2793.** The mutual inductance between two coils depends on their:
**Answer:** Factors like the number of turns, area, separation, and relative orientation of the coils determine how much of the flux from one coil links with the other.

**Q2794.** The magnetic field at the center of a circular current loop is:
**Answer:** The formula B = (μ₀I) / (2R) shows that the magnetic field is inversely proportional to the radius of the loop.

**Q2795.** An ideal step-down transformer decreases:
**Answer:** A step-down transformer is designed specifically to reduce the AC voltage.

**Q2796.** The presence of a magnetic field can be detected by a:
**Answer:** The needle of a magnetic compass is a small magnet that will align itself with external magnetic field lines.

**Q2797.** A proton (mass = 1.67 x 10⁻²⁷ kg, charge = 1.6 x 10⁻¹⁹ C) moves at 2 x 10⁶ m/s and enters a 0.1 T magnetic field at a right angle. What is the radius of its circular path?
**Answer:** Using r = mv/qB. r = (1.67 x 10⁻²⁷ \* 2 x 10⁶) / (1.6 x 10⁻¹⁹ \* 0.1) ≈ 0.21 m or 21 cm.

**Q2798.** For a DC motor, the current drawn is maximum when the motor has:
**Answer:** At startup, speed is zero, so back EMF is zero. With no opposition to the supply voltage, the current (I = V/R) is at its maximum.

**Q2799.** Ampere's Law is used to calculate:
**Answer:** Ampere's Law provides a powerful method for calculating the magnetic field for current distributions with high degrees of symmetry, like long wires and solenoids.

**Q2800.** An electron and a proton enter a magnetic field with the same velocity, perpendicular to the field. They will be deflected:
**Answer:** The radius of the circular path is r = mv/qB. Since the proton is much more massive, its radius will be larger. The direction of deflection will be opposite due to their opposite charges.

**Q2801.** The purpose of the split-ring commutator in a DC generator is to:
**Answer:** This action ensures that the current in the external circuit always flows in the same direction, converting the internally generated AC into a pulsating DC output.

**Q2802.** Magnetic lines of force:
**Answer:** This reflects the fact that there are no magnetic monopoles; every north pole is accompanied by a south pole.

**Q2803.** A 10 cm wire carrying a current of 5 A is placed in a magnetic field of 0.2 T. If the wire is perpendicular to the field, the force on the wire is:
**Answer:** Using F = ILBsin(θ), with L = 0.1 m and sin(90 degrees)=1, we get F = (5)(0.1)(0.2) = 0.1 N.

**Q2804.** The strength of the magnetic field is indicated by the:
**Answer:** Where the lines are closer together, the magnetic field is stronger. Where they are farther apart, the field is weaker.

**Q2805.** In a region where the magnetic field is zero, a moving charge will experience:
**Answer:** According to the Lorentz force equation, F = qvBsin(θ), if B=0, then F=0. No magnetic field means no magnetic force.

**Q2806.** The direction of the magnetic force on a positive charge is determined by the:
**Answer:** This rule correctly determines the direction of force on a positive charge moving in a magnetic field, where the thumb is velocity, fingers are the field, and the palm is the force.

**Q2807.** If a current-carrying solenoid is suspended freely, it will align itself in which direction?
**Answer:** A solenoid behaves like a bar magnet with a north and a south pole. It will align itself with the Earth's magnetic field lines.

**Q2808.** The sensitivity of a galvanometer is increased by decreasing the:
**Answer:** The torsional constant represents the stiffness of the suspension. A smaller 'c' means the coil twists more easily for a given torque, leading to higher sensitivity.

**Q2809.** A conductor of length 1 m moves at right angles to a magnetic field of 1 T with a velocity of 2 m/s. The induced EMF is:
**Answer:** Using the motional EMF formula E = BLv, we get E = (1)(1)(2) = 2 V.

**Q2810.** In a transformer, the primary coil has 100 turns and the secondary has 200 turns. If the input voltage is 120 V AC, the output voltage will be:
**Answer:** The voltage ratio is equal to the turns ratio: Vs/Vp = Ns/Np. So, Vs = 120 \* (200/100) = 240 V.

**Q2811.** Which of the following does NOT affect the magnitude of the induced EMF in a coil?
**Answer:** Resistance affects the magnitude of the induced \*current\* (I = E/R), but not the induced EMF (E) itself.

**Q2812.** A 'back current' that opposes the current supplied to a motor is generated by:
**Answer:** The rotation of the motor's coil in the magnetic field induces an EMF (back EMF) that opposes the supply voltage, thus creating a 'back current'.

**Q2813.** The unit of self-inductance, the Henry, is equivalent to:
**Answer:** From the formula E = -L (ΔI / Δt), we can rearrange to L = -E (Δt / ΔI), giving units of (Volt)(second)/Ampere.

**Q2814.** Two parallel wires carry currents in opposite directions. The magnetic field at a point midway between them is:
**Answer:** Using the right-hand grip rule, the field from the first wire and the field from the second wire are in the same direction at the midpoint, so they add together. Since the point is midway, the total field is double the field from one wire.

**Q2815.** The magnetic domain is a small region in which:
**Answer:** In ferromagnetic materials, atoms group into domains where their magnetic moments are aligned, creating a strong local magnetic field.

**Q2816.** What happens to the radius of a charged particle's circular path in a magnetic field if its kinetic energy is doubled?
**Answer:** Since radius r is proportional to velocity v, and velocity is proportional to the square root of kinetic energy (v is proportional to sqrt(K)), doubling K will multiply the radius by sqrt(2).

**Q2817.** The work done by a magnetic field on a moving charge is always:
**Answer:** The magnetic force is always perpendicular to the velocity of the charge. Since work done is W = Fdcos(θ) and θ = 90 degrees, the work done is zero. The magnetic field only changes the direction of the charge, not its speed or kinetic energy.

**Q2818.** An RLC circuit is in resonance. If the inductance L is doubled, what must happen to the capacitance C to maintain resonance?
**Answer:** The resonant frequency ω = 1/sqrt(LC). To keep ω constant, if L is doubled, C must be halved so that the product LC remains the same.

**Q2819.** In an AC generator, the induced EMF is maximum when the coil is:
**Answer:** When the plane of the coil is horizontal (moving parallel to the field), the sides of the coil are cutting the flux at the maximum rate.

**Q2820.** The core of a transformer is laminated to:
**Answer:** The insulating layers between the laminations break up the paths of induced currents (eddy currents), significantly reducing heat loss.

**Q2821.** A uniform magnetic field is directed into the page. An electron enters from the top and moves downwards. The force on the electron will be to the:
**Answer:** Using the right-hand rule for a positive charge gives a force to the right. Since an electron is negative, the force is in the opposite direction, to the left.

**Q2822.** The time period of a charged particle undergoing circular motion in a uniform magnetic field is independent of its:
**Answer:** The time period T = 2πr / v and the radius r = mv / qB. Substituting for r gives T = 2πm / qB. The velocity 'v' cancels out.

**Q2823.** Which device is a practical application of mutual induction?
**Answer:** A transformer relies on a changing current in one coil (the primary) inducing a changing magnetic flux and thus an EMF in a nearby second coil (the secondary).

**Q2824.** If you drop a bar magnet through a vertical copper tube, its acceleration will be:
**Answer:** As the magnet falls, it induces eddy currents in the copper tube. According to Lenz's law, these currents create a magnetic field that opposes the magnet's motion, resulting in a braking force and an acceleration less than g.

**Q2825.** A galvanometer has a resistance of G and a current Ig gives full-scale deflection. The shunt resistance S required to convert it into an ammeter of range I is:
**Answer:** The potential difference across the galvanometer (Ig \* G) must equal the potential difference across the shunt (Is \* S = (I - Ig) \* S). Rearranging for S gives the correct formula.

**Q2826.** The magnetic permeability of free space, μ₀, has a value of:
**Answer:** This is the defined value for the permeability of free space, fundamental to electromagnetism.

**Q2827.** In a CRT, the purpose of the grid is to:
**Answer:** The grid is made negative with respect to the cathode. By varying this negative potential, it controls the number of electrons passing through, thus controlling the brightness of the spot on the screen.

**Q2828.** If the current in a coil changes from 5 A to 2 A in 0.1 s, inducing an EMF of 3 V, the self-inductance of the coil is:
**Answer:** Using E = -L (ΔI / Δt), we have 3 = -L \* ((2-5) / 0.1) = -L \* (-3 / 0.1) = L \* (30). So, L = 3/30 = 0.1 H.

**Q2829.** The main difference between a DC generator and an AC generator is the:
**Answer:** An AC generator uses slip rings to maintain a continuous connection, producing an alternating current. A DC generator uses a split-ring commutator to reverse the connection every half turn, producing a direct current.

**Q2830.** A long straight wire carries a current. The magnetic field lines are:
**Answer:** The right-hand grip rule shows that the magnetic field lines form circles in a plane perpendicular to the wire.

**Q2831.** The torque on a current loop is zero when the angle between the magnetic field and the normal to the plane of the loop is:
**Answer:** The torque is given by τ = NIABsin(θ), where θ is the angle between the field and the normal. If θ = 0 degrees, sin(0 degrees)=0, so the torque is zero. The forces are pulling outwards, not causing rotation.

**Q2832.** A 50 cm long conductor moves at a constant speed of 4 m/s at right angles to a uniform magnetic field of 0.2 T. What is the motional EMF induced in the conductor?
**Answer:** Using E = BLv. E = (0.2 T) \* (0.5 m) \* (4 m/s) = 0.4 V.

**Q2833.** A changing magnetic flux of 1 Wb/s in a coil induces an EMF of 1 V. This is a statement of:
**Answer:** Faraday's Law of Induction, E = -N (dΦ/dt), directly relates the rate of change of magnetic flux to the induced EMF. For N=1, a rate of 1 Wb/s produces 1 V.

**Q2834.** If the current through an inductor is doubled, the energy stored in it:
**Answer:** The energy stored is given by E = 1/2 LI². If I is doubled, the energy becomes 1/2 L(2I)² = 4(1/2 LI²), which is four times the original energy.

**Q2835.** The operation of a metal detector is based on:
**Answer:** A metal detector uses a coil to generate a changing magnetic field. When a metal object is nearby, this field induces eddy currents in the metal. These currents, in turn, generate their own magnetic field that is detected by a second coil in the detector.

**Q2836.** In a velocity selector, the net force on a charged particle is zero. This means the magnetic force and the electric force are:
**Answer:** For the net force to be zero, the electric force (FE = qE) and the magnetic force (FB = qvB) must be equal in magnitude and point in opposite directions, so they cancel out.

**Q2837.** A transformer cannot work with a DC source because:
**Answer:** A steady DC current creates a constant magnetic field and thus a constant magnetic flux. Transformers require a \*changing\* magnetic flux to induce a voltage in the secondary coil, according to Faraday's Law.

**Q2838.** The SI unit for magnetic field strength (B) is the Tesla (T). An equivalent unit is:
**Answer:** From the force equation on a wire, F=ILB, we can rearrange to B=F/IL. The units are Newtons / (Amperes \* meters).

**Q2839.** A current-carrying loop is placed in a uniform magnetic field. The net magnetic force on the loop is:
**Answer:** In a uniform magnetic field, the forces on opposite sides of the loop are equal in magnitude and opposite in direction, so they cancel out, resulting in zero net force.

**Q2840.** The final image on the screen of a Cathode Ray Oscilloscope is:
**Answer:** The electron beam strikes a fluorescent screen, causing a single point to glow. This spot is then moved around by deflection plates to trace out a waveform.

**Q2841.** An ideal transformer with a primary voltage of 240 V has a secondary voltage of 12 V. If the current in the secondary coil is 2.0 A, what is the current in the primary coil?
**Answer:** For an ideal transformer, input power equals output power (VpIp = VsIs). So, Ip = (VsIs) / Vp = (12 V \* 2.0 A) / 240 V = 0.1 A.

**Q2842.** The phenomenon where a changing current in one coil induces an EMF in a neighboring coil is called:
**Answer:** This is the definition of mutual induction, the principle behind transformers.

**Q2843.** Increasing the frequency of AC supply to a transformer will:
**Answer:** The output voltage of an ideal transformer depends on the turns ratio and the input voltage, not the frequency.

**Q2844.** A wire loop is rotated in a uniform magnetic field. The frequency of rotation is doubled. The maximum induced EMF will:
**Answer:** The induced EMF in a generator is given by E = NBAωsin(ωt). Since angular frequency ω = 2πf, the maximum EMF (E\_max = NBAω) is directly proportional to the frequency. Doubling the frequency doubles the maximum EMF.

**Q2845.** Paramagnetic materials are weakly:
**Answer:** Paramagnetic materials have unpaired electrons that align with an external magnetic field, causing a weak attraction.

**Q2846.** A charge of 2.0 µC moves at a speed of 3 x 10⁶ m/s perpendicularly through a magnetic field of 0.5 T. What is the magnitude of the magnetic force on the charge?
**Answer:** Using F = qvBsin(θ), with sin(90°)=1. F = (2 x 10⁻⁶ C) \* (3 x 10⁶ m/s) \* (0.5 T) = 3.0 N.

**Q2847.** What is the function of the soft iron core in a moving coil galvanometer?
**Answer:** The soft iron core concentrates the magnetic field lines, making the field stronger. When shaped cylindrically, it also ensures the field is radial, meaning the torque is proportional to the current for a wider range of deflection.

**Q2848.** The power loss in a transformer due to the repeated magnetization and demagnetization of the core is called:
**Answer:** This energy loss is represented by the area of the material's hysteresis loop. It is the energy required to re-orient the magnetic domains in the core with each AC cycle.

**Q2849.** If a magnet is cut into two pieces, what is the result?
**Answer:** Cutting a magnet simply creates two smaller magnets, each complete with its own north and south pole.

**Q2850.** An ideal solenoid is one whose:
**Answer:** This condition ensures that the magnetic field inside is strong and uniform, and the field outside is negligible, which is the definition of an ideal solenoid.

**Q2851.** The output of a DC generator is a:
**Answer:** Due to the commutator action, the output voltage rises from zero to a maximum and back to zero, but always with the same polarity. This creates a pulsating DC.

**Q2852.** An inductor with an inductance of 80 mH carries a steady current of 5 A. How much energy is stored in its magnetic field?
**Answer:** Using E = 1/2 LI². E = 0.5 \* (80 x 10⁻³ H) \* (5 A)² = 0.5 \* 0.08 \* 25 = 1.0 J.

**Q2853.** Two inductors of inductance L₁ and L₂ are connected in series. The total inductance is:
**Answer:** Similar to resistors in series, inductances in series add up (assuming no mutual inductance).

**Q2854.** The study of charges at rest is called:
**Answer:** This branch of physics deals with the phenomena and properties of stationary or slow-moving electric charges with no acceleration.

**Q2855.** In a mass spectrometer, a charged particle's path radius is proportional to its:
**Answer:** The radius of the circular path is given by r = mv/qB. Therefore, for a given velocity, field, and charge, the radius is directly proportional to the mass. This allows separation of isotopes.

**Q2856.** A flat surface with an area of 0.2 m² is placed in a uniform magnetic field of 0.5 T. What is the magnetic flux through the area if the field is perpendicular to the surface?
**Answer:** Using Φ = BAcos(θ). Since the field is perpendicular to the surface, the angle θ with the normal is 0°, and cos(0°)=1. So, Φ = (0.5 T) \* (0.2 m²) = 0.1 Wb.

---

### Electronics (59 Questions)

**Q2857.** In a semiconductor, the forbidden energy gap is of the order of:
**Answer:** This value is characteristic of the energy required to move an electron from the valence band to the conduction band in a semiconductor.

**Q2858.** The process of adding impurities to a pure semiconductor is called:
**Answer:** This is the correct term for intentionally introducing impurities into a semiconductor to alter its electrical properties.

**Q2859.** In a p-type semiconductor, the majority charge carriers are:
**Answer:** P-type semiconductors are created by doping with trivalent impurities, which creates an excess of holes.

**Q2860.** The depletion region of a p-n junction is so named because it is depleted of:
**Answer:** This region is formed by the diffusion of electrons and holes across the junction, leaving behind immobile ions.

**Q2861.** When a p-n junction is forward biased, the width of the depletion region:
**Answer:** The applied forward voltage opposes the built-in potential, reducing the width of the depletion region and allowing current to flow.

**Q2862.** A diode is a device that allows current to flow in:
**Answer:** This is the defining characteristic of a diode, which acts as a one-way gate for current.

**Q2863.** The process of converting AC to DC is called:
**Answer:** This is the correct term for converting alternating current (AC) into direct current (DC).

**Q2864.** A Zener diode is designed to operate in the:
**Answer:** This is the region where the Zener diode maintains a constant voltage, making it useful for voltage regulation.

**Q2865.** A photodiode is operated in:
**Answer:** In reverse bias, the small reverse current is proportional to the intensity of light falling on the photodiode.

**Q2866.** The V-I characteristic curve for a silicon diode is shown. The point 'V\_k' on the graph represents the:
**Answer:** The point 'V\_k' where the current starts to increase rapidly in forward bias is called the knee voltage or cut-in voltage, which is approximately 0.7 V for silicon.

**Q2867.** When a transistor is used as a digital switch, it operates in which two regions?
**Answer:** As a switch, a transistor is either fully OFF (Cut-off region) or fully ON (Saturation region), representing the two states of a digital signal.

**Q2868.** In a common-emitter transistor, the emitter current (Ie) is 8.0 mA and the collector current (Ic) is 7.9 mA. What is the current gain (beta)?
**Answer:** First, find the base current: Ib = Ie - Ic = 8.0 - 7.9 = 0.1 mA. Then, calculate beta: β = Ic / Ib = 7.9 mA / 0.1 mA = 79.

**Q2869.** A transistor has how many doped regions?
**Answer:** A transistor consists of three doped regions: the emitter, base, and collector.

**Q2870.** In a PNP transistor, the charge carriers are mainly:
**Answer:** In a PNP transistor, the emitter and collector are p-type materials, so holes are the majority charge carriers.

**Q2871.** The base of a transistor is:
**Answer:** This design allows most of the charge carriers from the emitter to pass through to the collector.

**Q2872.** For a transistor to act as an amplifier, the emitter-base junction must be:
**Answer:** This is the standard biasing condition for a transistor to operate in the active region and function as an amplifier.

**Q2873.** The current gain of a common-emitter amplifier is given by the symbol:
**Answer:** Beta is the symbol for the current gain in a common-emitter configuration, defined as the ratio of collector current to base current.

**Q2874.** The relationship between alpha and beta is:
**Answer:** This is the correct relationship derived from the basic transistor current equations.

**Q2875.** What is the primary function of a capacitor placed in parallel with the load resistor in a rectifier circuit?
**Answer:** The capacitor acts as a filter. It charges up to the peak voltage of the rectifier's output and then discharges slowly through the load when the output drops, thus smoothing the DC signal.

**Q2876.** The conductivity of an intrinsic semiconductor increases with an increase in temperature primarily because:
**Answer:** Higher temperatures provide thermal energy to break covalent bonds, creating more free electrons and holes, which increases the density of charge carriers and thus conductivity.

**Q2877.** An ideal operational amplifier (Op-Amp) should have:
**Answer:** An ideal op-amp has infinite input impedance (so it draws no current from the source) and zero output impedance (so it can deliver maximum voltage to the load).

**Q2878.** An operational amplifier (op-amp) has a very high:
**Answer:** A high input impedance is a key characteristic of an op-amp, allowing it to draw very little current from the input source.

**Q2879.** In an inverting amplifier, the output signal is:
**Answer:** The inverting configuration of an op-amp produces an output that is a magnified and inverted version of the input.

**Q2880.** An oscillator is a circuit that produces:
**Answer:** An oscillator is a self-sustaining circuit that generates a repetitive output signal.

**Q2881.** Which of the following is a digital quantity?
**Answer:** This is a discrete, whole number, which is a digital quantity.

**Q2882.** The binary number system has a base of:
**Answer:** The binary system uses only two digits, 0 and 1, hence its base is 2.

**Q2883.** Which logic gate is known as the universal gate?
**Answer:** The NAND gate is a universal gate because any other logic function can be created using only NAND gates.

**Q2884.** The output of an AND gate is HIGH only when:
**Answer:** This is the defining characteristic of the AND gate.

**Q2885.** The Boolean expression for an OR gate is:
**Answer:** This represents the OR operation in Boolean algebra.

**Q2886.** A NOT gate is also known as an:
**Answer:** The NOT gate inverts the input signal, so it is also called an inverter.

**Q2887.** The ripple factor of a full-wave rectifier is:
**Answer:** This is the approximate ripple factor for a full-wave rectifier, which is much lower than that of a half-wave rectifier.

**Q2888.** In a common-base amplifier, the current gain alpha is always:
**Answer:** Alpha is the ratio of collector current to emitter current, and since some current is lost in the base, the collector current is always slightly less than the emitter current.

**Q2889.** The voltage gain of a non-inverting op-amp is given by:
**Answer:** This is the correct formula for the voltage gain of a non-inverting op-amp, which is always greater than or equal to 1.

**Q2890.** A bistable multivibrator is also known as a:
**Answer:** A flip-flop is a bistable multivibrator, meaning it has two stable states and can store one bit of information.

**Q2891.** The efficiency of a full-wave rectifier is approximately:
**Answer:** A full-wave rectifier is twice as efficient as a half-wave rectifier.

**Q2892.** The output of a NOR gate is HIGH only when:
**Answer:** This is the only condition under which the output of a NOR gate is HIGH.

**Q2893.** The decimal equivalent of the binary number 1011 is:
**Answer:** The calculation is (1 x 2^3) + (0 x 2^2) + (1 x 2^1) + (1 x 2^0) = 8 + 0 + 2 + 1 = 11.

**Q2894.** In a common-emitter amplifier, the output voltage is taken from the:
**Answer:** The output is taken from the collector terminal.

**Q2895.** An LED (Light Emitting Diode) emits light when:
**Answer:** When forward biased, electrons and holes recombine at the junction, releasing energy in the form of photons (light).

**Q2896.** A transistor can be used as a:
**Answer:** Depending on its biasing, a transistor can function as either a switch or an amplifier.

**Q2897.** The output of an XOR gate is HIGH when:
**Answer:** The XOR (Exclusive OR) gate gives a HIGH output only when the inputs are different.

**Q2898.** The potential barrier for silicon at room temperature is approximately:
**Answer:** This is the typical value for the potential barrier of a silicon p-n junction.

**Q2899.** A filter circuit is used to:
**Answer:** A filter, typically a capacitor, is used to remove the AC components (ripples) from the rectifier's output.

**Q2900.** The gain of an amplifier without feedback is known as:
**Answer:** This is the term for the intrinsic gain of the amplifier itself, without any feedback circuit.

**Q2901.** In a bridge rectifier, how many diodes are used?
**Answer:** A bridge rectifier uses four diodes arranged in a bridge configuration to achieve full-wave rectification without a center-tapped transformer.

**Q2902.** Zener breakdown in a p-n junction occurs due to:
**Answer:** Zener breakdown happens in heavily doped diodes with a narrow depletion region, where the intense electric field is strong enough to directly break covalent bonds and create carriers.

**Q2903.** A logic circuit consists of a two-input AND gate followed by a NOT gate. This combination is equivalent to a single:
**Answer:** The function of an AND gate is Y = A.B. Inverting this output with a NOT gate gives Y = (A.B)', which is the definition of a NAND gate.

**Q2904.** What is the result of the binary addition 1101 + 0110?
**Answer:** Starting from the right: 1+0=1. 0+1=1. 1+1=0 with a carry of 1. 1+0+1(carry)=0 with a carry of 1. The final result is 10011.

**Q2905.** In the circuit shown, if the input is a sine wave, which component is primarily responsible for converting the pulsating DC into a smoother DC?
**Answer:** The diode (D1) performs rectification (AC to pulsating DC). The capacitor (C) acts as a filter, smoothing the pulsating DC by storing and releasing charge, thereby reducing the ripple.

**Q2906.** The Boolean expression A + A'B is equivalent to:
**Answer:** This is the correct simplification using the rules of Boolean algebra: A + A'B = (A + A')(A + B) = 1(A + B) = A + B.

**Q2907.** The collector of a transistor is:
**Answer:** The collector is moderately doped to reduce its resistance and is the largest region to dissipate heat.

**Q2908.** An op-amp can be used as a comparator to:
**Answer:** In its open-loop configuration, an op-amp's output saturates to one of its supply voltages depending on which input has a higher potential.

**Q2909.** De Morgan's first theorem states that (AB)' is equal to:
**Answer:** This is the correct statement of De Morgan's first theorem, which relates the NAND and OR operations.

**Q2910.** The resistance of a reverse-biased p-n junction is:
**Answer:** The wide depletion region in a reverse-biased junction offers a very high resistance to current flow.

**Q2911.** In a conductor, the valence band and conduction band:
**Answer:** The overlapping of these bands allows electrons to move freely, resulting in high conductivity.

**Q2912.** The number of diodes required in a center-tapped full-wave rectifier is:
**Answer:** This type of rectifier uses two diodes, one for each half of the AC cycle.

**Q2913.** Which of the following is an active component?
**Answer:** A transistor is an active component because it can amplify a signal, which requires an external power source.

**Q2914.** The phase difference between the input and output of a common-emitter amplifier is:
**Answer:** The common-emitter configuration inverts the input signal, resulting in a 180-degree phase shift.

**Q2915.** A device that converts electrical energy into light energy is:
**Answer:** An LED (Light Emitting Diode) is specifically designed for this energy conversion.

---

### Electrostatics (103 Questions)

**Q2916.** Coulomb's law is most similar to:
**Answer:** Both Coulomb's law and Newton's law of universal gravitation are inverse-square laws, meaning the force is inversely proportional to the square of the distance between the interacting objects.

**Q2917.** The constant of proportionality, k, in Coulomb's law depends on:
**Answer:** The value of the Coulomb constant (k) is dependent on the permittivity of the medium. Its value is highest in a vacuum.

**Q2918.** The unit of electric charge is the:
**Answer:** The Coulomb (C) is the standard SI unit for electric charge, named after Charles-Augustin de Coulomb.

**Q2919.** The number of electric field lines passing through a certain area is a measure of the:
**Answer:** Electric flux is defined as the measure of the flow of the electric field through a given area. It's proportional to the number of field lines passing through it.

**Q2920.** Gauss's law relates the electric flux through a closed surface to the:
**Answer:** Gauss's law states that the net electric flux through any hypothetical closed surface is equal to 1/ε₀ times the net electric charge enclosed within that closed surface.

**Q2921.** The electric potential at a point is defined as the work done in bringing a unit positive charge from:
**Answer:** Electric potential is the work done per unit charge to move a test charge from a reference point (usually infinity) to a specific point in an electric field.

**Q2922.** The unit of electric potential is the:
**Answer:** The volt (V) is the SI unit for electric potential, potential difference, and electromotive force. It is defined as one joule per coulomb (J/C).

**Q2923.** An equipotential surface is a surface where:
**Answer:** An equipotential surface is a surface on which all points have the same electric potential. 'Equi' means equal, so it's a surface of equal potential.

**Q2924.** Two point charges, +4 μC and -6 μC, are separated by 20 cm in a vacuum. What is the magnitude of the electrostatic force between them?
**Answer:** Use Coulomb's Law: F = k \* |q1 \* q2| / r². First, convert units: q1 = 4 x 10⁻⁶ C, q2 = -6 x 10⁻⁶ C, and r = 0.2 m. Then, F = (9 x 10⁹) \* |(4 x 10⁻⁶) \* (-6 x 10⁻⁶)| / (0.2)² = (9 x 10⁹) \* (24 x 10⁻¹²) / 0.04 = 5.4 N.

**Q2925.** The work done in moving a charge between two points on an equipotential surface is:
**Answer:** Since the potential difference between any two points on an equipotential surface is zero, the work done (W = qΔV) is also zero.

**Q2926.** A capacitor is a device used to:
**Answer:** A capacitor is a passive electronic component that stores electrical energy in an electric field by accumulating electric charges on two close conductors.

**Q2927.** The capacitance of a parallel plate capacitor is directly proportional to the:
**Answer:** The formula C = εA/d shows that capacitance (C) is directly proportional to the plate area (A) and inversely proportional to the distance (d) between them.

**Q2928.** The unit of capacitance is the:
**Answer:** The Farad (F), named after Michael Faraday, is the SI unit of capacitance. One Farad is defined as one Coulomb per Volt (C/V).

**Q2929.** When capacitors are connected in series, the total capacitance is:
**Answer:** For capacitors in series, the reciprocal of the total capacitance is the sum of the reciprocals of the individual capacitances (1/C\_total = 1/C1 + 1/C2 + ...), resulting in a total capacitance smaller than any individual one.

**Q2930.** When capacitors are connected in parallel, the total capacitance is:
**Answer:** For capacitors in parallel, the total capacitance is the sum of the individual capacitances (C\_total = C1 + C2 + ...), which is always greater than the largest individual capacitance.

**Q2931.** A uniform electric field of 500 N/C is directed downwards. What is the magnitude of the force on a proton placed in this field?
**Answer:** Use the formula F = q \* E. The charge on a proton is the elementary charge, q = 1.6 x 10⁻¹⁹ C. So, F = (1.6 x 10⁻¹⁹ C) \* (500 N/C) = 800 x 10⁻¹⁹ N, which is 8.0 x 10⁻¹⁷ N.

**Q2932.** The energy stored in a capacitor is proportional to the:
**Answer:** The energy stored in a capacitor can be expressed as U = Q²/2C or U = ½CV². In both forms, it's proportional to the square of the charge (Q) or the square of the voltage (V).

**Q2933.** A dielectric material is an:
**Answer:** A dielectric is an electrical insulator that can be polarized by an applied electric field. When placed in a capacitor, it increases the capacitance.

**Q2934.** When a dielectric material is inserted between the plates of a capacitor, the capacitance:
**Answer:** The dielectric material reduces the electric field strength, allowing more charge to be stored at the same potential difference, thus increasing the capacitance by a factor K (the dielectric constant).

**Q2935.** The electric field inside a charged spherical conductor is:
**Answer:** In electrostatic equilibrium, the net electric field inside any conductor is zero. Any field would cause the free charges to move until the field is canceled out.

**Q2936.** The electric potential inside a charged spherical conductor is:
**Answer:** Since the electric field inside is zero, no work is done moving a charge from the surface to any point inside. Therefore, the potential is constant throughout the conductor and equal to the potential at its surface.

**Q2937.** Which of the following is a vector quantity?
**Answer:** An electric field has both magnitude (strength) and direction, making it a vector quantity. The others are scalar quantities.

**Q2938.** The force between two charges is 120 N. If the distance between the charges is doubled, the force will be:
**Answer:** According to Coulomb's law, force is inversely proportional to the square of the distance (F ∝ 1/r²). If the distance is doubled (2r), the force becomes 1/(2r)² = 1/4 of the original force. 120 N / 4 = 30 N.

**Q2939.** Two charges of +2 μC and +5 μC are separated by a distance. The ratio of the forces exerted by the charges on each other will be:
**Answer:** According to Newton's third law, the forces two objects exert on each other are always equal in magnitude and opposite in direction. This action-reaction pair applies to electrostatic forces as well.

**Q2940.** The SI unit of permittivity of free space (ε₀) is:
**Answer:** This unit can be derived from the Coulomb's law formula, F = (1/4πε₀)(q₁q₂/r²). Rearranging for ε₀ gives its units as C² / (N·m²).

**Q2941.** A capacitor of 10 μF and another of 20 μF are connected in parallel to a 6 V source. What is the total charge stored by the combination?
**Answer:** First, find the equivalent capacitance for a parallel combination: C\_eq = C1 + C2 = 10 μF + 20 μF = 30 μF. Then, find the total charge using Q\_total = C\_eq \* V = (30 x 10⁻⁶ F) \* (6 V) = 180 x 10⁻⁶ C, or 180 μC.

**Q2942.** An electron is placed in a uniform electric field. The electron will experience a force:
**Answer:** The force on a charge is F = qE. Since an electron's charge (q) is negative, the force vector will be in the opposite direction to the electric field vector.

**Q2943.** A hollow metallic sphere is given a positive charge. The electric potential inside the sphere will be:
**Answer:** The potential inside a charged conductor is constant and equal to the potential on its surface. Since the charge is positive, the potential will be positive.

**Q2944.** The process of charging a conductor by bringing it near another charged object without touching it is called:
**Answer:** Charging by induction involves redistributing the charge on an object by bringing a charged object nearby, and then using a ground connection to remove some of that charge.

**Q2945.** A gold-leaf electroscope is used to:
**Answer:** An electroscope is a simple device used to detect the presence and sign of an electric charge by observing the repulsion of its thin metallic leaves.

**Q2946.** The work done in moving a charge of 4 C from a point at 10 V to a point at 15 V is:
**Answer:** Work done (W) is the charge (q) multiplied by the potential difference (ΔV). W = q(V\_final - V\_initial) = 4 C \* (15 V - 10 V) = 4 C \* 5 V = 20 J.

**Q2947.** The electric potential energy of a system of two charges is positive if the charges are:
**Answer:** Positive potential energy implies a repulsive force. Work must be done on the system to bring two like charges (both positive or both negative) together. Option 2 is also correct, but typically only one is listed.

**Q2948.** The electric potential due to a point charge is inversely proportional to the:
**Answer:** The formula for electric potential is V = kq/r. This shows that potential (V) is inversely proportional to the distance (r) from the point charge.

**Q2949.** The capacitance of a capacitor is 1 F. This means that the capacitor can store 1 coulomb of charge when the potential difference across it is:
**Answer:** By definition, capacitance is C = Q/V. If C = 1 F and Q = 1 C, then V must be 1 V.

**Q2950.** A photocopier works on the principle of:
**Answer:** Photocopiers use a process called xerography, which relies on electrostatics to attract charged toner particles to a photosensitive drum that has been exposed to an image.

**Q2951.** What is the magnitude of the electric field at a point 3 meters away from a point charge of +2 nC?
**Answer:** Use the electric field formula: E = k \* |q| / r². Convert charge to Coulombs: q = 2 x 10⁻⁹ C. Then, E = (9 x 10⁹) \* (2 x 10⁻⁹) / 3² = (9 x 10⁹) \* (2 x 10⁻⁹) / 9 = 2 N/C.

**Q2952.** The relative permittivity of a medium is the ratio of the permittivity of the medium to the:
**Answer:** Relative permittivity (or dielectric constant) K = ε / ε₀, where ε is the permittivity of the medium and ε₀ is the permittivity of free space (a vacuum).

**Q2953.** How much energy is stored in a 200 μF capacitor that is charged to a potential difference of 50 V?
**Answer:** Use the energy formula for a capacitor: U = 0.5 \* C \* V². Convert capacitance to Farads: C = 200 x 10⁻⁶ F. Then, U = 0.5 \* (200 x 10⁻⁶ F) \* (50 V)² = 0.5 \* (200 x 10⁻⁶) \* 2500 = 0.25 J.

**Q2954.** The dielectric constant of a conductor is:
**Answer:** Since the electric field inside a conductor is zero, the dielectric constant, which represents the factor by which an external field is reduced, is considered to be infinite.

**Q2955.** Two parallel plates have an electric field of 1000 N/C between them. If the distance between the plates is 0.02 m, the potential difference between the plates is:
**Answer:** For a uniform electric field, the potential difference is ΔV = E \* d. So, ΔV = 1000 N/C \* 0.02 m = 20 V.

**Q2956.** The SI unit of electric flux is:
**Answer:** Electric flux (Φ) is the product of the electric field (E, in N/C) and the perpendicular area (A, in m²). Thus, its unit is N·m²/C. Option 3, V·m, is also a correct unit for electric flux.

**Q2957.** A lightning rod works by:
**Answer:** A lightning rod provides a low-resistance path to the ground. The sharp point concentrates the electric field, ionizing the air and creating a conductive path for the lightning strike to follow safely to the ground.

**Q2958.** The electric field is strongest where the electric field lines are:
**Answer:** The density of electric field lines (how close they are to each other) in a diagram represents the strength of the electric field. Closer lines indicate a stronger field.

**Q2959.** A Millikan oil drop experiment is used to determine the:
**Answer:** Robert Millikan's experiment involved balancing the gravitational and electric forces on tiny charged oil droplets to calculate the charge of a single electron, proving that charge is quantized.

**Q2960.** The charge on an electron is approximately:
**Answer:** The elementary charge, which is the charge of a single proton or the magnitude of the charge of a single electron, is approximately 1.602 x 10⁻¹⁹ Coulombs.

**Q2961.** A material that has a large number of free electrons is a good:
**Answer:** Good electrical conductors, like metals, have a sea of delocalized electrons that are free to move throughout the material, allowing for the easy flow of electric current.

**Q2962.** The process of connecting a charged object to the Earth to neutralize it is called:
**Answer:** Grounding (or earthing) provides a path for charge to flow to or from the Earth, which acts as a massive reservoir of charge, effectively neutralizing the object.

**Q2963.** Three capacitors of 2 F, 3 F, and 6 F are connected in series. The equivalent capacitance is:
**Answer:** For series capacitors, 1/C\_eq = 1/C₁ + 1/C₂ + 1/C₃ = 1/2 + 1/3 + 1/6 = (3+2+1)/6 = 6/6 = 1. Therefore, C\_eq = 1 F.

**Q2964.** The space between the plates of a parallel plate capacitor is filled with a dielectric of constant K. The capacitance is increased by a factor of:
**Answer:** The capacitance of a capacitor with a dielectric is C' = K \* C, where C is the original capacitance and K is the dielectric constant of the material.

**Q2965.** The electric field lines and equipotential surfaces are always:
**Answer:** The electric field vector at any point is always perpendicular to the equipotential surface passing through that point. This is because no work is done moving along an equipotential surface.

**Q2966.** Two parallel plates are separated by 2 cm and have a potential difference of 40 V. What is the magnitude of the uniform electric field between them?
**Answer:** For a uniform field, E = V / d. Convert distance to meters: d = 0.02 m. Then, E = 40 V / 0.02 m = 2000 N/C.

**Q2967.** If the distance between two point charges is halved, the electric force between them becomes:
**Answer:** Force is proportional to 1/r². If r becomes r/2, the new force is proportional to 1/(r/2)², which is 1/(r²/4) or 4/r². The force becomes four times larger.

**Q2968.** The presence of a dielectric between the plates of a capacitor reduces the:
**Answer:** The polarized dielectric creates an internal electric field that opposes the external field from the plates, reducing the net electric field between them.

**Q2969.** A Van de Graaff generator is a device that can:
**Answer:** It works by using a moving belt to accumulate electric charge on a hollow metal globe, building up a very high electrostatic potential (voltage).

**Q2970.** The concept of electric field lines was introduced by:
**Answer:** Michael Faraday, a brilliant experimentalist, introduced the concept of lines of force as a visual and conceptual tool to understand electric and magnetic phenomena.

**Q2971.** The relationship between electric field (E) and electric potential (V) is given by:
**Answer:** The electric field is the negative gradient of the electric potential. This means the field points in the direction of the steepest decrease in potential. E = V/d is a special case for uniform fields.

**Q2972.** An electric dipole consists of two:
**Answer:** This is the precise definition of an electric dipole, a common configuration found in molecules like water.

**Q2973.** A 5 μF capacitor is connected to a 12 V battery. How much charge is stored on the capacitor's plates?
**Answer:** Use the capacitance formula: Q = C \* V. First, convert capacitance to Farads: C = 5 x 10⁻⁶ F. Then, Q = (5 x 10⁻⁶ F) \* (12 V) = 60 x 10⁻⁶ C, which is 60 μC.

**Q2974.** The torque experienced by an electric dipole in a uniform electric field is maximum when the angle between the dipole moment and the electric field is:
**Answer:** The torque is given by τ = pE sin(θ). The sine function is maximum (value of 1) when the angle θ is 90 degrees, meaning the dipole is perpendicular to the field.

**Q2975.** The potential energy of an electric dipole in a uniform electric field is minimum when the angle between the dipole moment and the electric field is:
**Answer:** Potential energy is U = -pE cos(θ). The cosine function is maximum (value of 1) at 0 degrees, making the potential energy most negative (minimum). This is the stable equilibrium position.

**Q2976.** A Gaussian surface is an imaginary closed surface used in conjunction with:
**Answer:** Gauss's Law requires the use of a hypothetical closed surface, called a Gaussian surface, to relate the flux through it to the charge enclosed within it.

**Q2977.** The electric flux through a closed surface enclosing no net charge is:
**Answer:** According to Gauss's Law, the net flux is proportional to the enclosed charge (Φ = Q\_enc / ε₀). If the enclosed charge is zero, the net flux must also be zero.

**Q2978.** The work done by the electric field in moving a positive charge from a point of high potential to a point of low potential is:
**Answer:** A positive charge naturally moves from high potential to low potential, so the field does positive work, and the charge loses potential energy.

**Q2979.** One electron-volt (eV) is a unit of:
**Answer:** An electron-volt is the amount of kinetic energy gained by a single electron when it accelerates through a potential difference of one volt. 1 eV = 1.602 x 10⁻¹⁹ Joules.

**Q2980.** The capacitance of the Earth, considered as a spherical conductor of radius 6400 km, is approximately:
**Answer:** Using the formula for the capacitance of an isolated sphere, C = 4πε₀R, the value is approximately 711 x 10⁻⁶ Farads, or 711 microfarads.

**Q2981.** In a region where the electric field is uniform, the equipotential surfaces are:
**Answer:** For a uniform electric field, where the field lines are parallel and equally spaced, the equipotential surfaces are planes that are perpendicular to the field lines.

**Q2982.** The phenomenon of an uncharged body getting attracted towards a charged body is known as:
**Answer:** The charged body induces a separation of charge (polarization) in the neutral object, bringing opposite charges closer and like charges farther away, resulting in a net attractive force.

**Q2983.** The time constant of an RC circuit is a measure of the time taken:
**Answer:** The time constant (τ = RC) represents the time required for the voltage across a charging capacitor to reach approximately 1 - 1/e, or 63.2%, of its final value.

**Q2984.** When a soap bubble is given a negative charge, its radius:
**Answer:** The negative charges distribute over the surface and repel each other. This mutual repulsion creates an outward pressure that causes the soap bubble to expand.

**Q2985.** Quantization of charge means that:
**Answer:** The principle of quantization states that any observable charge is an integer multiple of the elementary charge, e (the charge of one electron or proton).

**Q2986.** Dielectric strength is the maximum:
**Answer:** Dielectric strength is an intrinsic property of an insulating material that defines the maximum electric field it can sustain before it loses its insulating properties and becomes conductive (breaks down).

**Q2987.** The SI unit of electric dipole moment is:
**Answer:** The electric dipole moment (p) is a vector quantity defined as p = qd, where q is the magnitude of the charge and d is the separation distance. Its unit is therefore Coulomb times meter.

**Q2988.** How much work is required to move a +3 nC charge from a point with a potential of 20 V to a point with a potential of 80 V?
**Answer:** Use the work-potential formula: W = q \* (V\_final - V\_initial). Here, q = 3 x 10⁻⁹ C and the potential difference is 80 V - 20 V = 60 V. So, W = (3 x 10⁻⁹ C) \* (60 V) = 180 x 10⁻⁹ J, or 180 nJ.

**Q2989.** The electric field due to an infinite plane sheet of charge is:
**Answer:** Using Gauss's Law, it can be shown that the electric field from an infinite sheet of charge is uniform (E = σ/2ε₀), and thus does not change with distance from the sheet.

**Q2990.** A capacitor blocks DC but allows AC to pass. This is because:
**Answer:** Capacitive reactance is Xc = 1/(2πfC). For DC, frequency f=0, so Xc is infinite (acting as an open circuit). For AC, f > 0, so Xc is finite, allowing current to 'pass'.

**Q2991.** The surface charge density is defined as:
**Answer:** Surface charge density (σ) is used to describe the distribution of charge over a surface and is calculated as σ = Q/A, where Q is the total charge and A is the surface area.

**Q2992.** Two capacitors of capacitance C1 and C2 are connected in parallel. If a charge Q is given to the combination, the charge will be distributed as:
**Answer:** In a parallel connection, the voltage (V) across both capacitors is the same. Since Q = CV, we have Q₁ = C₁V and Q₂ = C₂V. The ratio Q₁/Q₂ = (C₁V)/(C₂V) = C₁/C₂.

**Q2993.** The speed of light 'c' is related to the permittivity (ε₀) and permeability (μ₀) of free space by the equation:
**Answer:** This fundamental equation, derived from Maxwell's equations, links the constants of electricity (ε₀) and magnetism (μ₀) to the speed of light, showing that light is an electromagnetic wave.

**Q2994.** A Faraday cage is a container made of conducting material that:
**Answer:** A Faraday cage works because an external electric field causes the charges within the conductor to redistribute, creating an opposing field that exactly cancels the external field inside the cage.

**Q2995.** The energy density (energy per unit volume) in an electric field is:
**Answer:** The energy density stored in an electric field is given by u = ½ε₀E². This shows that energy is stored in the field itself and its density is proportional to the square of the field strength.

**Q2996.** If a glass rod is rubbed with silk, the glass rod becomes positively charged. This is because:
**Answer:** In triboelectric charging, certain materials have a higher affinity for electrons. Silk has a higher affinity than glass, so it pulls electrons from the glass, leaving the glass with a net positive charge.

**Q2997.** The force per unit charge is known as:
**Answer:** This is the definition of electric field strength (E = F/q). It describes the force that would be exerted on a standard test charge at a certain point in space.

**Q2998.** A charged particle moving in a uniform electric field will follow a path that is a:
**Answer:** The particle experiences a constant force (and thus constant acceleration) in one direction, while its velocity component perpendicular to the field remains constant. This is analogous to projectile motion under gravity, which results in a parabolic path.

**Q2999.** The electric field lines due to a positive point charge are:
**Answer:** By convention, field lines show the direction of force on a positive test charge. A positive point charge would repel a positive test charge, so the field lines point radially away from it in all directions.

**Q3000.** The process of an insulator becoming a conductor under a strong electric field is called:
**Answer:** When the electric field is strong enough (exceeding the material's dielectric strength), it can rip electrons from their atoms, creating free charges and causing the insulator to conduct current. This is dielectric breakdown.

**Q3001.** Three capacitors of equal capacitance C are connected in series. The equivalent capacitance is:
**Answer:** For series capacitors: 1/C\_eq = 1/C + 1/C + 1/C = 3/C. Inverting this gives C\_eq = C/3.

**Q3002.** Three capacitors of equal capacitance C are connected in parallel. The equivalent capacitance is:
**Answer:** For parallel capacitors, the equivalent capacitance is the sum of the individual capacitances: C\_eq = C + C + C = 3C.

**Q3003.** A charged capacitor is connected to a resistor. The charge on the capacitor will:
**Answer:** This describes the discharging process of an RC circuit. The rate of discharge is proportional to the remaining charge, which mathematically leads to an exponential decay.

**Q3004.** What is the electric potential at a distance of 50 cm from a point charge of -8 nC?
**Answer:** Use the electric potential formula: V = k \* q / r. Convert units: q = -8 x 10⁻⁹ C and r = 0.5 m. Then, V = (9 x 10⁹) \* (-8 x 10⁻⁹) / 0.5 = -72 / 0.5 = -144 V.

**Q3005.** An electroscope can be charged by:
**Answer:** It can be charged by direct contact with a charged object (conduction) or by using a nearby charged object and a ground connection to induce a net charge (induction).

**Q3006.** If you are in a car during a thunderstorm, you are safe from lightning because of the:
**Answer:** The car's metal body acts as a Faraday cage, causing the electric charge from the lightning to flow around the outside of the vehicle to the ground, protecting the occupants inside.

**Q3007.** The electric field inside a charged parallel plate capacitor is:
**Answer:** Away from the edges, the electric field lines between two large parallel plates are parallel and equally spaced, indicating a uniform field strength and direction.

**Q3008.** A material with a high dielectric constant is a good:
**Answer:** A high dielectric constant indicates that a material is a very good insulator that can effectively reduce the electric field passing through it, making it ideal for use in capacitors.

**Q3009.** The total energy of an isolated system of charges is:
**Answer:** The principle of conservation of energy applies to all isolated systems, including systems of electric charges. Energy can change form (e.g., potential to kinetic), but the total amount remains constant.

**Q3010.** The electric force is a conservative force. This means that:
**Answer:** This is the definition of a conservative force. The work done in moving a charge between two points depends only on the start and end points, not the path followed, which allows for the definition of electric potential energy.

**Q3011.** In a uniform electric field, the potential difference between two points is proportional to the:
**Answer:** The potential difference is ΔV = -E⋅d, where d is the displacement vector. The potential changes only when moving parallel to the field lines.

**Q3012.** The electric field lines can never cross each other. This is because:
**Answer:** The electric field at any single point in space must have a unique direction and magnitude. If lines crossed, it would imply two different directions for the force at that single point, which is a physical impossibility.

**Q3013.** The process of charging a body by rubbing is called:
**Answer:** Triboelectric charging, or charging by friction, is the process where electrons are transferred from one material to another through rubbing, based on their electron affinity.

**Q3014.** A good conductor has a:
**Answer:** Resistivity is the measure of how strongly a material opposes the flow of electric current. Good conductors, like copper, have very low resistivity.

**Q3015.** The SI unit of resistivity is:
**Answer:** Resistivity (ρ) is an intrinsic property of a material. It relates resistance (R), length (L), and cross-sectional area (A) by the formula R = ρL/A, giving it units of Ω·m.

**Q3016.** Corona discharge is the phenomenon of:
**Answer:** At sharp points on a conductor, the charge concentration is very high, creating an intense electric field that can be strong enough to ionize the surrounding air or fluid, causing a discharge.

**Q3017.** Three capacitors with capacitances 2 F, 4 F, and 4 F are connected in series. What is the equivalent capacitance of the combination?
**Answer:** For capacitors in series, the formula is 1/C\_eq = 1/C1 + 1/C2 + 1/C3. So, 1/C\_eq = 1/2 + 1/4 + 1/4 = 0.5 + 0.25 + 0.25 = 1. Therefore, C\_eq = 1 F.

**Q3018.** Which of the following materials is a good dielectric?
**Answer:** Mica is a stable mineral that is an excellent electrical insulator with a high dielectric strength, making it a common choice for use in capacitors and other electronic components.
