### Nuclear Physics (109 Questions)

**Q3108.** The nucleus of an atom consists of:
**Answer:** The atomic nucleus is made of protons and neutrons, collectively called nucleons. Electrons orbit outside the nucleus.

**Q3109.** Isotopes are atoms of the same element with:
**Answer:** Isotopes have the same atomic number (protons) but different mass numbers due to a varying number of neutrons.

**Q3110.** The mass of a neutron is approximately:
**Answer:** The mass of a neutron is approximately 1.675 x 10⁻²⁷ kg, which is very close to the mass of a proton.

**Q3111.** One atomic mass unit (amu) is equivalent to:
**Answer:** According to Einstein's mass-energy equivalence (E=mc²), 1 amu of mass is equivalent to 931.5 MeV of energy.

**Q3112.** The 'mass defect' in a nucleus is the:
**Answer:** The mass of a stable nucleus is always slightly less than the sum of the masses of its individual protons and neutrons. This 'missing' mass is converted into binding energy.

**Q3113.** The force that holds the nucleons together in a nucleus is called the:
**Answer:** The strong nuclear force is the fundamental force that binds protons and neutrons together, overcoming the electrostatic repulsion between protons.

**Q3114.** Which of the following has the highest penetrating power?
**Answer:** Gamma rays are high-energy photons with no mass or charge, allowing them to penetrate matter much more deeply than charged particles like alpha or beta.

**Q3115.** An alpha particle is essentially a nucleus of:
**Answer:** An alpha particle consists of two protons and two neutrons, which is the structure of a Helium-4 nucleus.

**Q3116.** In beta decay, a neutron is converted into:
**Answer:** Negative beta decay involves a neutron changing into a proton, emitting an electron (the beta particle) and an antineutrino to conserve energy and lepton number.

**Q3117.** The half-life of a radioactive element is the time in which:
**Answer:** Half-life is a statistical measure defining the time it takes for 50% of a given sample of radioactive nuclei to undergo decay.

**Q3118.** A sample contains 5 x 10¹² radioactive atoms. If its decay constant is 4 x 10⁻⁸ s⁻¹, what is the initial activity of the sample in Bq?
**Answer:** Activity (A) is the product of the decay constant (λ) and the number of radioactive atoms (N). A = λN = (4×10⁻⁸ s⁻¹) × (5×10¹²) = 20×10⁴ Bq = 2.0 x 10⁵ Bq.

**Q3119.** The half-life of Technetium-99m is 6 hours. What percentage of a sample remains undecayed after one full day (24 hours)?
**Answer:** 24 hours corresponds to 4 half-lives (24/6 = 4). The fraction remaining is (1/2)⁴ = 1/16. In percentage, 1/16 is 6.25%.

**Q3120.** The SI unit of radioactivity is the:
**Answer:** The Becquerel (Bq) is the SI unit, defined as one decay per second. The Curie is an older, non-SI unit.

**Q3121.** A Geiger-Müller counter is used to detect:
**Answer:** A G-M counter works by detecting the ionization of gas inside its tube caused by the passage of radiation like alpha, beta, or gamma particles.

**Q3122.** The process of splitting a heavy nucleus into two lighter nuclei is called:
**Answer:** Fission is the splitting of a large atomic nucleus into smaller ones, a process that releases a large amount of energy.

**Q3123.** In a nuclear reactor, the moderator is used to:
**Answer:** Moderators (like heavy water or graphite) slow down the high-energy neutrons produced by fission, making them more likely to be captured by other U-235 nuclei and sustain the chain reaction.

**Q3124.** Which of the following is commonly used as a moderator in a nuclear reactor?
**Answer:** Heavy water (D₂O) and graphite are common moderators because they are effective at slowing neutrons without absorbing them excessively.

**Q3125.** Nuclear fusion is the process in which:
**Answer:** Fusion involves the combining of light nuclei, like hydrogen isotopes, into a heavier nucleus, releasing immense energy. It is the process that powers stars.

**Q3126.** The energy of the sun is primarily a result of:
**Answer:** The Sun's core has extremely high temperature and pressure, allowing hydrogen nuclei to fuse into helium, releasing the energy we receive as sunlight.

**Q3127.** The Wilson cloud chamber is used to:
**Answer:** In a cloud chamber, charged particles leave trails of condensation in a supersaturated vapor, making their paths visible for study.

**Q3128.** A solid-state detector is essentially a:
**Answer:** Solid-state detectors use a semiconductor diode (a p-n junction) where radiation creates electron-hole pairs, generating a detectable electrical pulse.

**Q3129.** In the nuclear reaction U-235 + n -> Ba-141 + Kr-92 + 3n, the process is:
**Answer:** This reaction shows a heavy Uranium nucleus splitting into two smaller nuclei (Barium and Krypton) and releasing more neutrons, which is the definition of nuclear fission.

**Q3130.** The binding energy per nucleon is maximum for:
**Answer:** The peak of the binding energy curve is around Iron-56, making it the most stable nucleus. Fission and fusion both release energy by moving towards this peak.

**Q3131.** A radioactive sample has a half-life of 20 minutes. What fraction of the sample will be left undecayed after 1 hour?
**Answer:** One hour contains 60 minutes, which is equal to three half-lives (60/20 = 3). The fraction remaining after 'n' half-lives is (1/2)ⁿ. So, after 3 half-lives, the fraction is (1/2)³ = 1/8.

**Q3132.** Gamma rays are:
**Answer:** Gamma rays are a form of electromagnetic radiation (photons) with very high energy, no mass, and no charge.

**Q3133.** The charge of an alpha particle is:
**Answer:** An alpha particle consists of two protons (each with charge +e) and two neutrons (no charge), giving it a total charge of +2e.

**Q3134.** The half-life of carbon-14 is approximately:
**Answer:** The half-life of Carbon-14 is about 5730 years, which makes it suitable for dating ancient organic materials.

**Q3135.** Which of the following is not a fundamental force of nature?
**Answer:** Friction is an emergent force resulting from electromagnetic interactions between surfaces. The four fundamental forces are Strong, Weak, Electromagnetic, and Gravitational.

**Q3136.** The phenomenon of natural radioactivity was discovered by:
**Answer:** Henri Becquerel discovered radioactivity in 1896 when he observed that uranium salts could expose a photographic plate without any light source.

**Q3137.** In the equation E = mc², 'c' represents:
**Answer:** In Einstein's famous mass-energy equivalence equation, 'c' is the constant representing the speed of light in a vacuum (approximately 3 x 10⁸ m/s).

**Q3138.** The control rods in a nuclear reactor are typically made of:
**Answer:** Control rods are made of materials that are strong neutron absorbers, like Cadmium or Boron, to regulate or shut down the fission chain reaction.

**Q3139.** When a nucleus emits a gamma ray, its atomic number:
**Answer:** Gamma decay is the release of energy from an excited nucleus. No protons or neutrons are lost, so both the atomic number (Z) and mass number (A) remain unchanged.

**Q3140.** After two half-lives, the fraction of the original radioactive sample that remains is:
**Answer:** After one half-life, 1/2 remains. After a second half-life, half of the remaining sample decays, so (1/2) \* (1/2) = 1/4 remains.

**Q3141.** The decay constant (λ) is related to the half-life (T₁/₂) by the equation:
**Answer:** The decay constant (λ), which represents the probability of decay per unit time, is inversely proportional to the half-life. The constant 0.693 is the natural logarithm of 2.

**Q3142.** The 'dead time' of a Geiger-Müller counter is the time:
**Answer:** After detecting a particle, the G-M tube needs a brief recovery period to reset before it can accurately detect the next one. This period of insensitivity is the dead time.

**Q3143.** In a fusion reaction, the mass of the products is:
**Answer:** Similar to fission, the fusion process converts a small amount of mass into a large amount of energy, so the resulting nucleus has less mass than the sum of the initial nuclei.

**Q3144.** The range of the strong nuclear force is approximately:
**Answer:** The strong force is extremely powerful but only acts over very short distances, typically the diameter of a small nucleus (around 1 femtometer or 10⁻¹⁵ m).

**Q3145.** An antineutrino is emitted during:
**Answer:** In negative beta decay, a neutron becomes a proton, and an electron and an antineutrino are emitted to conserve lepton number.

**Q3146.** A chain reaction in a nuclear reactor is sustained by:
**Answer:** The fission of a uranium nucleus releases several neutrons, which then go on to cause other uranium nuclei to undergo fission, creating a self-sustaining chain reaction.

**Q3147.** The principle of a solid-state detector is based on the creation of:
**Answer:** When radiation strikes the semiconductor material in a solid-state detector, it excites electrons, creating mobile electron-hole pairs that produce a measurable electrical signal.

**Q3148.** The study of the age of geological samples can be done using:
**Answer:** Carbon dating is used for organic remains (up to ~50,000 years), while Uranium dating, with its much longer half-life, is used for rocks and geological formations.

**Q3149.** Which of the following particles has no charge and no mass (or very little mass)?
**Answer:** Neutrinos are fundamental particles that are electrically neutral and have a very small, but non-zero, mass. They interact very weakly with other matter.

**Q3150.** The unit 'roentgen' is used to measure:
**Answer:** The Roentgen is a unit of radiation exposure, defined by the amount of ionization it causes in a specific amount of air.

**Q3151.** A tracer is a:
**Answer:** A radioactive tracer is an isotope that is attached to a compound and introduced into a system (like the human body) to follow its path by detecting the emitted radiation.

**Q3152.** In the reaction ¹⁴N + ⁴He -> X + ¹H, the nucleus X is:
**Answer:** To conserve mass and atomic numbers: Mass number A = (14+4) - 1 = 17. Atomic number Z = (7+2) - 1 = 8. The element with Z=8 is Oxygen (O).

**Q3153.** The binding energy per nucleon curve shows that:
**Answer:** Both processes move nuclei towards the most stable region of the curve (around Iron), thus releasing energy. Lighter nuclei fuse and heavier nuclei split to become more stable.

**Q3154.** Which of the following is the most stable nucleus?
**Answer:** Iron-56 sits at the peak of the binding energy per nucleon curve, meaning it is the most stable and tightly bound nucleus.

**Q3155.** Background radiation is due to:
**Answer:** Natural background radiation is the sum of radiation from all these sources: cosmic rays from space, terrestrial sources from the ground, and internal sources within our bodies.

**Q3156.** Pair production is the conversion of:
**Answer:** When a high-energy photon (gamma ray) with at least 1.022 MeV passes near a nucleus, its energy can be converted into the mass of an electron and its antiparticle, a positron.

**Q3157.** The critical mass is the minimum mass of a fissile material required to:
**Answer:** For a chain reaction to be self-sustaining, at least one neutron from each fission must cause another fission. Below the critical mass, too many neutrons escape the material.

**Q3158.** In radiotherapy, cancer cells are destroyed by:
**Answer:** Radiotherapy uses high-energy radiation to damage the DNA of cancerous cells, disrupting their ability to reproduce and leading to their death.

**Q3159.** The atomic number (Z) of a nucleus represents the number of:
**Answer:** The atomic number Z is the defining characteristic of a chemical element and is equal to the number of protons in its nucleus.

**Q3160.** Which statement is true for the strong nuclear force?
**Answer:** The strong force acts equally between proton-proton, neutron-neutron, and proton-neutron pairs (charge-independent) but only over the very small distance of the nucleus.

**Q3161.** A nucleus of thorium ²³²Th decays to radium ²²⁸Ra. What type of decay is this?
**Answer:** The mass number decreases by 4 (232-228) and the atomic number decreases by 2 (90-88), which corresponds to the emission of an alpha particle (a Helium nucleus).

**Q3162.** The half-life of a radioactive substance is 10 days. What will be the amount left after 30 days if the initial amount is 8 grams?
**Answer:** 30 days is equal to three half-lives (30/10 = 3). After 1st half-life: 4g. After 2nd: 2g. After 3rd: 1g.

**Q3163.** The ratio of the mass numbers of two nuclei is 64:27. What is the ratio of their nuclear radii?
**Answer:** The nuclear radius R is proportional to the cube root of the mass number A (R ∝ A¹/³). Therefore, the ratio of the radii is (64/27)¹/³ = 4/3 or 4:3.

**Q3164.** The activity of a radioactive sample is 800 Bq. If its half-life is 5 days, what will its activity be after 20 days?
**Answer:** 20 days is equal to four half-lives (20/5 = 4). After each half-life, the activity is halved. So, the final activity will be 800 × (1/2)⁴ = 800 / 16 = 50 Bq.

**Q3165.** The mean life (τ) of a radioactive nuclide is related to its decay constant (λ) by:
**Answer:** The mean life (average lifetime) of a nucleus is the reciprocal of its decay constant. It is slightly longer than the half-life.

**Q3166.** For a nuclear fusion reaction to occur, the temperature and pressure must be extremely high in order to:
**Answer:** Nuclei are positively charged and repel each other. High temperature gives them enough kinetic energy to overcome this electrostatic repulsion and get close enough for the strong force to fuse them.

**Q3167.** Which of the following is NOT a component of a nuclear power reactor?
**Answer:** Particle accelerators are used in research to study particles, but are not part of a commercial nuclear reactor, which relies on a self-sustaining chain reaction.

**Q3168.** In a Geiger-Müller counter, the gas used is typically:
**Answer:** An inert gas like Argon is used because it is easily ionized. A quenching agent (like alcohol vapor) is added to stop the discharge after each detection.

**Q3169.** The 'quenching' process in a G-M tube is necessary to:
**Answer:** Quenching absorbs excess energy and positive ions, preventing the tube from firing continuously after a single radiation event, thus resetting it for the next detection.

**Q3170.** Annihilation of matter occurs when:
**Answer:** When a particle (like an electron) and its antiparticle (a positron) meet, they annihilate each other, converting their entire mass into energy, typically in the form of two gamma-ray photons.

**Q3171.** A radioactive tracer used in medical imaging should ideally have:
**Answer:** A short half-life ensures it decays quickly and doesn't remain in the body. Gamma rays are used because they can easily exit the body to be detected by external cameras.

**Q3172.** Which of the following correctly lists radiation types in order of increasing ionizing power?
**Answer:** Ionizing power is the ability to knock electrons off atoms. Large, highly charged alpha particles are the most ionizing, while uncharged gamma rays are the least.

**Q3173.** The concept of a nucleus was first proposed by Rutherford based on his:
**Answer:** In the gold foil experiment, the unexpected deflection of some alpha particles led Rutherford to conclude that the atom's positive charge and mass are concentrated in a small, dense nucleus.

**Q3174.** The half-life of a radioactive nucleus is 200 seconds. What is its approximate mean life (average lifetime)?
**Answer:** The mean life (τ) is related to the half-life (T₁/₂) by τ ≈ 1.44 × T₁/₂. So, τ ≈ 1.44 × 200 s = 288.6 s.

**Q3175.** What fraction of a radioactive sample remains after 9 years if its half-life is 3 years?
**Answer:** The time elapsed is 9 years, which is 3 half-lives (9/3 = 3). The fraction remaining is (1/2)³ = 1/8.

**Q3176.** The activity of a sample is 1.2 x 10⁶ Bq, and its decay constant is 3.0 x 10⁻⁹ s⁻¹. Approximately how many radioactive atoms are present in the sample?
**Answer:** From the activity formula A = λN, the number of atoms is N = A / λ. So, N = (1.2 x 10⁶ Bq) / (3.0 x 10⁻⁹ s⁻¹) = 0.4 x 10¹⁵ = 4.0 x 10¹⁴ atoms.

**Q3177.** If the decay constant of a radioactive sample is large, its half-life is:
**Answer:** The decay constant represents the probability of decay. A higher probability (large constant) means the substance decays quickly, resulting in a short half-life.

**Q3178.** A 'thermal' neutron is a neutron that has:
**Answer:** Thermal neutrons are in thermal equilibrium with their surroundings. These slow-moving neutrons have a much higher probability of being captured by a U-235 nucleus.

**Q3179.** The energy released in a nuclear reaction corresponds to the:
**Answer:** According to E=mc², any energy released (or absorbed) during a reaction is directly proportional to the change (decrease or increase) in the total mass of the particles.

**Q3180.** The penetration of radiation through matter is a process that is:
**Answer:** Whether a single particle of radiation interacts with matter is a matter of probability. We can predict the behavior of large numbers of particles but not a single one.

**Q3181.** Which particle is its own antiparticle?
**Answer:** The photon, the quantum of light, has no charge or other quantum numbers that would distinguish it from its antiparticle. Therefore, it is its own antiparticle.

**Q3182.** The fuel in most common types of nuclear reactors is:
**Answer:** Uranium-235 is the primary fissile isotope used in most commercial nuclear reactors. Natural uranium is enriched to increase the concentration of U-235.

**Q3183.** What happens to the neutron-to-proton ratio of a nucleus after it undergoes negative beta decay?
**Answer:** In negative beta decay, a neutron (n) turns into a proton (p). This decreases the number of neutrons and increases the number of protons, thus decreasing the n/p ratio.

**Q3184.** A breeder reactor is designed to:
**Answer:** Breeder reactors are designed to convert fertile material (like U-238) into fissile material (like Pu-239) at a rate faster than they consume their own fissile fuel.

**Q3185.** A radioactive source with a half-life of 28 years has an activity of 1000 Bq. How many years will it take for its activity to reduce to 125 Bq?
**Answer:** The activity reduces from 1000 Bq to 125 Bq. 1000 → 500 → 250 → 125. This is 3 steps, meaning 3 half-lives have passed. Total time = 3 × 28 years = 84 years.

**Q3186.** A stationary nucleus of ²³⁸U decays, releasing 4.27 MeV of energy. What is the approximate kinetic energy of the resulting alpha particle?
**Answer:** By conservation of momentum, the KE of the alpha particle is KE\_α = Q × (A-4)/A, where A is the mass number of the parent. KE\_α = 4.27 MeV × (234/238) ≈ 4.20 MeV.

**Q3187.** The visible tracks in a Wilson cloud chamber are made of:
**Answer:** A charged particle ionizes the vapor as it passes through. These ions then act as nucleation sites for tiny droplets of liquid to condense, forming a visible trail.

**Q3188.** The atomic mass unit (amu) is defined as:
**Answer:** By international agreement, the standard for the atomic mass unit is based on the Carbon-12 atom, which is defined as having a mass of exactly 12 amu.

**Q3189.** In the notation ᴬzX, the neutron number N is given by:
**Answer:** The mass number (A) is the total number of nucleons (protons + neutrons), and the atomic number (Z) is the number of protons. Therefore, the number of neutrons is the difference: A - Z.

**Q3190.** Radioactive decay is a spontaneous process, which means:
**Answer:** Spontaneous decay means it occurs randomly for any given nucleus and cannot be sped up or slowed down by external factors like temperature, pressure, or chemical environment.

**Q3191.** In a nuclear reaction, the total mass of reactants is 4.0028 amu and the total mass of products is 4.0010 amu. How much energy is released in the reaction?
**Answer:** The energy released (Q-value) is the mass difference (Δm) multiplied by 931.5 MeV. Δm = 4.0028 - 4.0010 = 0.0018 amu. So, E = 0.0018 × 931.5 ≈ 1.677 MeV.

**Q3192.** The binding energy of a Lithium nucleus (⁷₃Li) is 39.2 MeV. What is its approximate binding energy per nucleon?
**Answer:** The binding energy per nucleon is the total binding energy divided by the mass number (A). For Lithium-7, A=7. So, 39.2 MeV / 7 nucleons = 5.6 MeV/nucleon.

**Q3193.** Which of the following is a 'lepton'?
**Answer:** Leptons are a class of fundamental particles that do not experience the strong nuclear force. The electron, muon, tau, and their corresponding neutrinos are all leptons.

**Q3194.** The activity of a radioactive sample is measured in Becquerels (Bq). This is a measure of:
**Answer:** Activity is defined as the number of nuclear decays per unit time. One Becquerel (Bq) is equal to one decay per second.

**Q3195.** In the core of the sun, the proton-proton cycle results in the formation of:
**Answer:** The primary fusion process in the Sun is the proton-proton chain, in which four hydrogen nuclei (protons) are ultimately converted into one helium nucleus, releasing energy.

**Q3196.** Which detector would be most suitable for accurately measuring the energy of incoming radiation?
**Answer:** Solid-state detectors produce a signal whose size is proportional to the energy deposited by the radiation, allowing for precise energy measurement (spectroscopy). G-M counters cannot do this.

**Q3197.** A nucleus of ²¹⁰Po decays into ²⁰⁶Pb. How many alpha and beta decays have occurred?
**Answer:** The mass number changes by 4, so there is 1 alpha decay (¹⁰Po -> ²⁰⁶X). This would make Z=82. The daughter is Pb (Z=82), but the parent Po is Z=84. **Q3198.** The biological effect of radiation is measured in which unit?
**Answer:** The Sievert (Sv) is the unit of equivalent dose, which accounts for both the amount of energy deposited (in Grays) and the biological effectiveness of the type of radiation.

**Q3199.** A stable nucleus generally has:
**Answer:** For light elements, the stable ratio is ~1:1. For heavier elements, more neutrons are needed to counteract the increasing electrostatic repulsion between protons, so the n/p ratio increases.

**Q3200.** The force carrier (exchange particle) for the electromagnetic force is the:
**Answer:** The photon is the quantum of the electromagnetic field and mediates the electromagnetic force between charged particles. Gluons mediate the strong force.

**Q3201.** Nuclear isomers are nuclei that have the same A and Z but differ in their:
**Answer:** Isomers are nuclei with the same number of protons and neutrons but are in different excited, metastable energy states. They decay via gamma emission or internal conversion.

**Q3202.** The minimum energy required for a photon to cause pair production is:
**Answer:** This energy must be at least the sum of the rest mass energies of the electron (0.511 MeV) and the positron (0.511 MeV), which totals 1.022 MeV.

**Q3203.** Which of the following processes is responsible for element-building (nucleosynthesis) in stars heavier than the sun?
**Answer:** The CNO (Carbon-Nitrogen-Oxygen) cycle is a catalytic fusion process that is the dominant source of energy in stars more massive than our Sun.

**Q3204.** The 'coolant' in a nuclear reactor is a substance used to:
**Answer:** The immense heat generated by fission in the reactor core is carried away by a coolant (like water, gas, or liquid metal) to a heat exchanger to produce steam for turbines.

**Q3205.** If a nucleus captures an electron from an inner atomic shell, the process is called:
**Answer:** In electron capture, a proton-rich nucleus captures one of its own atomic electrons, converting a proton into a neutron and emitting a neutrino.

**Q3206.** Which of the following statements about neutrinos is FALSE?
**Answer:** According to the theory of relativity, no particle with mass can travel at or faster than the speed of light. All other statements about neutrinos are true.

**Q3207.** The binding energy per nucleon curve's shape is due to the interplay between:
**Answer:** The short-range strong force provides binding, while the long-range electromagnetic repulsion between protons tries to push the nucleus apart. This opposition shapes the curve.

**Q3208.** In a bubble chamber, the paths of particles are seen as:
**Answer:** A bubble chamber contains a superheated liquid. A charged particle passing through creates ions that trigger the formation of tiny bubbles, leaving a visible track.

**Q3209.** The half-life of Radon-222 is 3.8 days. A sample will be reduced to 1/16th of its original amount in:
**Answer:** To get to 1/16th, four half-lives must pass (1/2 -> 1/4 -> 1/8 -> 1/16). So, the total time is 4 \* 3.8 days = 15.2 days.

**Q3210.** Which particle completes this nuclear reaction: ⁹Be + ⁴He -> ¹²C + ?
**Answer:** To balance the equation: Mass number A = (9+4) - 12 = 1. Atomic number Z = (4+2) - 6 = 0. A particle with A=1 and Z=0 is a neutron.

**Q3211.** Radioisotopes used in smoke detectors, such as Americium-241, typically emit:
**Answer:** Americium-241 emits alpha particles, which ionize the air in a small chamber. Smoke entering the chamber disrupts this ionization, triggering the alarm.

**Q3212.** The term 'nucleon' refers to:
**Answer:** Nucleon is the collective name for the main constituents of the atomic nucleus: protons and neutrons.

**Q3213.** The half-life of a radioisotope is 10 hours. How long will it take for 87.5% of the sample to decay?
**Answer:** If 87.5% of the sample has decayed, 12.5% (or 1/8) remains. Since 1/8 = (1/2)³, three half-lives have passed. Total time = 3 × 10 hours = 30 hours.

**Q3214.** Why is the energy spectrum of beta particles continuous?
**Answer:** In beta decay, the total available energy is shared in a variable ratio between the emitted electron and the antineutrino, resulting in a continuous energy spectrum for the electron.

**Q3215.** The purpose of a radiation shield around a nuclear reactor is primarily to absorb:
**Answer:** Thick layers of concrete and lead are used to absorb the intense neutron and gamma radiation produced in the reactor core, protecting workers and the environment.

**Q3216.** If the radius of a nucleus with A=3 is R, what will be the approximate radius of a nucleus with A=81?
**Answer:** The nuclear radius is proportional to the cube root of the mass number (A¹/³). The ratio of the mass numbers is 81/3 = 27. The cube root of 27 is 3. Therefore, the new radius will be 3R.

---

### Rotational And Circular Motion (95 Questions)

**Q3217.** The relationship between linear velocity (v) and angular velocity (ω) is:
 **A.** v = ω/r
 **B.** v = r/ω
 **C.** v = r²ω
 **D.** v = rω ✓
**Answer:** v = rω

**Q3218.** Centripetal acceleration is always directed:
 **A.** Tangent to the circular path
 **B.** Away from the center
 **C.** In the direction of velocity
 **D.** Towards the center ✓
**Answer:** Towards the center

**Q3219.** The rotational analogue of mass is:
 **A.** Angular momentum
 **B.** Angular velocity
 **C.** Moment of inertia ✓
**Answer:** Moment of inertia

**Q3220.** Torque is defined as the cross product of:
 **A.** Force and velocity
 **B.** Mass and angular acceleration
 **C.** Force and position vector
 **D.** Position vector and force ✓
**Answer:** Position vector and force

**Q3221.** If no external torque acts on a system, which quantity is conserved?
 **A.** Kinetic energy
 **B.** Angular velocity
 **C.** Moment of inertia
 **D.** Angular momentum ✓
**Answer:** Angular momentum

**Q3222.** A geostationary satellite orbits the Earth. What is its angular velocity?
 **A.** Equal to the Sun's angular velocity
 **B.** Greater than the Earth's angular velocity
 **C.** Equal to the Earth's angular velocity ✓
**Answer:** Equal to the Earth's angular velocity

**Q3223.** The time period of a geostationary satellite is:
 **A.** 1 hour
 **B.** 30 days
 **C.** 365 days
 **D.** 24 hours ✓
**Answer:** 24 hours

**Q3224.** Artificial gravity in a space station can be created by:
 **A.** Increasing the station's mass
 **B.** Moving at high speed
 **C.** Decreasing the air pressure
 **D.** Rotating the station ✓
**Answer:** Rotating the station

**Q3225.** The direction of angular velocity is determined by the:
 **A.** Left-hand rule
 **B.** Law of gravitation
 **C.** Newton's second law
 **D.** Right-hand rule ✓
**Answer:** Right-hand rule

**Q3226.** For a rigid body rotating with constant angular velocity, its angular acceleration is:
 **A.** Constant but non-zero
 **B.** Zero ✓
**Answer:** Zero

**Q3227.** The force required to keep an object moving in a circle is called:
 **A.** Centrifugal force
 **B.** Gravitational force
 **C.** Frictional force
 **D.** Centripetal force ✓
**Answer:** Centripetal force

**Q3228.** Moment of inertia depends on the mass of the body and the:
 **A.** Angular velocity
 **B.** Applied torque
 **C.** Linear velocity
 **D.** Distribution of mass about the axis ✓
**Answer:** Distribution of mass about the axis

**Q3229.** Which has a larger moment of inertia: a solid sphere or a hollow sphere of the same mass and radius?
 **A.** Solid sphere
 **B.** They have the same moment of inertia
 **C.** Depends on the angular velocity
 **D.** Hollow sphere ✓
**Answer:** Hollow sphere

**Q3230.** The dimensions of angular momentum are:
 **A.** [MLT⁻¹]
 **B.** [ML²T⁻²]
 **C.** [MT⁻²]
 **D.** [ML²T⁻¹] ✓
**Answer:** [ML²T⁻¹]

**Q3231.** Orbital velocity of a satellite close to the Earth is approximately:
 **A.** 11.2 km/s
 **B.** 9.8 m/s
 **C.** 3.0 × 10⁸ m/s
 **D.** 7.9 km/s ✓
**Answer:** 7.9 km/s

**Q3232.** Weightlessness experienced by an astronaut in orbit is due to:
 **A.** Zero gravity at that altitude
 **B.** The spacecraft shielding from gravity
 **C.** The high speed of the spacecraft
 **D.** The astronaut and spacecraft being in constant free-fall ✓
**Answer:** The astronaut and spacecraft being in constant free-fall

**Q3233.** For a car on a banked curve (angle θ), the centripetal force is provided by:
 **A.** The car's weight
 **B.** The force of friction only
 **C.** The vertical component of the normal force
 **D.** The horizontal component of the normal force ✓
**Answer:** The horizontal component of the normal force

**Q3234.** What is the angular speed of the minute hand of a clock in rad/s?
 **A.** π/3600
 **B.** π/60
 **C.** 2π
 **D.** π/1800 ✓
**Answer:** π/1800

**Q3235.** A flywheel gains a speed of 540 rpm in 6 seconds. Its angular acceleration is:
 **A.** 6π rad/s²
 **B.** 9π rad/s²
 **C.** 18π rad/s²
 **D.** 3π rad/s² ✓
**Answer:** 3π rad/s²

**Q3236.** The equations of rotational motion are valid only for:
 **A.** Constant angular velocity
 **B.** Variable angular acceleration
 **C.** Any rotational motion
 **D.** Constant angular acceleration ✓
**Answer:** Constant angular acceleration

 **A.** Decreases

**Answer:** Increases

**Answer:** A force on the agent providing the centripetal force

---

### Thermodynamics (102 Questions)

**Q3312.** What is the study of the relationships between heat, work, and energy called?
**Answer:** Thermodynamics is the branch of physics that deals with heat, work, and temperature, and their relation to energy, radiation, and the physical properties of matter.

**Q3313.** Which of the following is a statement of the First Law of Thermodynamics?
**Answer:** This is the principle of conservation of energy, which is the foundation of the First Law of Thermodynamics.

**Q3314.** What is the internal energy of an ideal gas dependent on?
**Answer:** For an ideal gas, the internal energy is the sum of the kinetic energies of its molecules, which is directly proportional to the absolute temperature.

**Q3315.** In an isothermal process, which of the following remains constant?
**Answer:** The prefix 'iso-' means equal, and 'thermal' relates to heat or temperature.

**Q3316.** In an adiabatic process, what is true about the heat transfer?
**Answer:** An adiabatic process is defined as one in which no heat is transferred into or out of the system (Q=0).

**Q3317.** What is the work done by a gas during an isobaric expansion?
**Answer:** For a constant pressure (isobaric) process, the work done is the product of the constant pressure and the change in volume.

**Q3318.** The efficiency of a Carnot engine depends on:
**Answer:** The efficiency of an ideal Carnot engine is determined solely by the absolute temperatures of the heat source (hot reservoir) and heat sink (cold reservoir).

**Q3319.** What is the measure of the disorder or randomness of a system called?
**Answer:** Entropy is a thermodynamic property that is a measure of the molecular disorder or randomness of a system.

**Q3320.** According to the Second Law of Thermodynamics, the entropy of an isolated system:
**Answer:** The Second Law states that the total entropy of an isolated system can never decrease over time; it increases for irreversible (natural) processes and remains constant for reversible processes.

**Q3321.** What is the SI unit of entropy?
**Answer:** Entropy is defined as heat divided by temperature, so its units are energy per temperature (J/K).

**Q3322.** A refrigerator is an example of a:
**Answer:** A refrigerator is a specific type of heat pump that moves heat from a cold space (inside the fridge) to a warmer space (the room).

**Q3323.** If 8400 J of heat is added to a 2 kg block of copper, raising its temperature from 10°C to 20°C, what is the specific heat capacity of copper?
**Answer:** The formula for heat transfer is Q = mcΔT. The temperature change ΔT is 20°C - 10°C = 10°C (which is a change of 10 K). Rearranging to find c: c = Q / (mΔT) = 8400 J / (2 kg \* 10 K) = 420 J/(kg·K).

**Q3324.** The molar specific heat at constant pressure (Cₚ) is always greater than the molar specific heat at constant volume (Cᵥ) for an ideal gas because:
**Answer:** At constant pressure, the supplied heat energy is used for both increasing the internal energy and doing work of expansion. At constant volume, all the heat goes into increasing the internal energy.

**Q3325.** The relationship between Cₚ and Cᵥ for an ideal gas is:
**Answer:** This is Mayer's formula, which relates the two molar specific heats to the ideal gas constant R.

**Q3326.** The triple point of water is:
**Answer:** The triple point is a unique condition of temperature and pressure where all three phases (solid, liquid, gas) of a substance can coexist in thermodynamic equilibrium.

**Q3327.** Which of the following processes is irreversible?
**Answer:** Once gases are mixed through diffusion, they will not spontaneously separate. This is an example of an irreversible process that increases entropy.

**Q3328.** The work done in a cyclic process is equal to:
**Answer:** The net work done during a cyclic process is represented by the area inside the loop on a pressure-volume (P-V) diagram.

**Q3329.** A heat engine takes in 100 J of heat from a hot reservoir and exhausts 60 J of heat to a cold reservoir. What is its efficiency?
**Answer:** Efficiency = (Work output / Heat input). Work output = Heat input - Heat output = 100 J - 60 J = 40 J. So, efficiency = (40 J / 100 J) \* 100% = 40%.

**Q3330.** The equation of state for an ideal gas is:
**Answer:** This is the ideal gas law, which relates pressure (P), volume (V), number of moles (n), the ideal gas constant (R), and temperature (T).

**Q3331.** The zeroth law of thermodynamics deals with:
**Answer:** The zeroth law states that if two systems are each in thermal equilibrium with a third system, then they are in thermal equilibrium with each other. This establishes the concept of temperature.

**Q3332.** The specific heat capacity of a substance is the amount of heat required to raise the temperature of:
**Answer:** This is the definition of specific heat capacity, an intensive property of a substance.

**Q3333.** What is the process called when a substance changes directly from a solid to a gas?
**Answer:** Sublimation is the phase transition of a substance directly from the solid to the gas state, without passing through the liquid state.

**Q3334.** The latent heat of fusion is the heat required to:
**Answer:** The term 'fusion' refers to melting. Latent heat is the energy absorbed or released during a phase change at a constant temperature.

**Q3335.** Which of the following is an extensive property?
**Answer:** An extensive property is a property that depends on the amount of matter in a sample. Volume, mass, and internal energy are extensive.

**Q3336.** Which of the following is an intensive property?
**Answer:** An intensive property does not depend on the amount of matter. Density, temperature, and pressure are intensive.

**Q3337.** The efficiency of a practical heat engine is always:
**Answer:** The Carnot engine represents the maximum possible efficiency for any heat engine operating between two given temperatures, due to its ideal, reversible nature.

**Q3338.** The first law of thermodynamics is a statement of:
**Answer:** The first law is a version of the law of conservation of energy, adapted for thermodynamic systems, relating internal energy, heat, and work.

**Q3339.** In an isochoric process, what remains constant?
**Answer:** The term 'isochoric' comes from the Greek words 'isos' (equal) and 'khora' (space or volume).

**Q3340.** In an isobaric process, what remains constant?
**Answer:** The term 'isobaric' comes from the Greek words 'isos' (equal) and 'baros' (weight or pressure).

**Q3341.** The change in internal energy of a system in a cyclic process is:
**Answer:** Internal energy is a state function. Since a cyclic process returns the system to its initial state, the net change in internal energy is zero.

**Q3342.** Heat can be transferred by:
**Answer:** These are the three fundamental modes of heat transfer.

**Q3343.** The transfer of heat by the movement of a fluid is called:
**Answer:** Convection is the heat transfer due to the bulk movement of molecules within fluids such as gases and liquids.

**Q3344.** The transfer of heat through electromagnetic waves is called:
**Answer:** Thermal radiation is the emission of energy as electromagnetic waves. It does not require a medium to travel.

**Q3345.** The transfer of heat through direct contact is called:
**Answer:** Conduction is the process where heat is transmitted through a substance from a region of higher temperature to a region of lower temperature without any movement of the material itself.

**Q3346.** The absolute zero of temperature is:
**Answer:** Absolute zero (0 Kelvin) is the lowest possible temperature, where all classical molecular motion ceases.

**Q3347.** The work done on a system is considered:
**Answer:** By physics sign convention for the first law (ΔU = Q - W), work done on a system (compression) is negative, as it increases the internal energy.

**Q3348.** The work done by a system is considered:
**Answer:** By physics sign convention for the first law (ΔU = Q - W), work done by a system (expansion) is positive, as it decreases the internal energy.

**Q3349.** The heat absorbed by a system is considered:
**Answer:** By convention, heat absorbed by a system (endothermic) is positive, as it increases the internal energy.

**Q3350.** The heat released by a system is considered:
**Answer:** By convention, heat released by a system (exothermic) is negative, as it decreases the internal energy.

**Q3351.** The coefficient of performance of a refrigerator is given by:
**Answer:** The coefficient of performance (COP) is the ratio of the desired output (heat removed from the cold reservoir, Qc) to the required input (work done, W).

**Q3352.** The coefficient of performance of a heat pump is given by:
**Answer:** The coefficient of performance (COP) for a heat pump is the ratio of the desired output (heat delivered to the hot reservoir, Qh) to the required input (work done, W).

**Q3353.** A Carnot engine operates between a hot reservoir at 327°C and a cold reservoir at 27°C. What is its theoretical maximum efficiency?
**Answer:** First, convert temperatures to Kelvin: Tₕ = 327 + 273 = 600 K; T꜀ = 27 + 273 = 300 K. The efficiency (η) is calculated using the formula η = 1 - (T꜀ / Tₕ). So, η = 1 - (300 K / 600 K) = 1 - 0.5 = 0.5, which is 50%.

**Q3354.** A process that can be reversed without leaving any change in the system or the surroundings is called:
**Answer:** This is the definition of a reversible process, which is an idealization used in thermodynamics.

**Q3355.** Which of the following is an example of a state function?
**Answer:** A state function (like internal energy, pressure, temperature, volume) depends only on the current state of the system, not on the path taken to reach that state.

**Q3356.** Which of the following is an example of a path function?
**Answer:** A path function (like heat and work) is a property that depends on the path taken to get from an initial to a final state.

**Q3357.** The third law of thermodynamics states that the entropy of a perfect crystal at absolute zero is:
**Answer:** This is the statement of the third law. At absolute zero, a perfect crystal has a minimum possible entropy, which is defined as zero.

**Q3358.** A system that can exchange both energy and matter with its surroundings is called:
**Answer:** An example is a pot of boiling water without a lid, which exchanges heat (energy) and water vapor (matter) with the air.

**Q3359.** A system that can exchange energy but not matter with its surroundings is called:
**Answer:** An example is a sealed can of soup being heated. It can absorb heat (energy) but no matter can enter or leave.

**Q3360.** A system that cannot exchange either energy or matter with its surroundings is called:
**Answer:** An example is a perfectly insulated thermos. It is an idealization as perfect isolation is not achievable in practice.

**Q3361.** The internal energy of a system can be changed by:
**Answer:** The first law of thermodynamics (ΔU = Q - W) shows that the change in internal energy (ΔU) is the sum of heat added (Q) and work done on the system (-W).

**Q3362.** The temperature of a substance is a measure of the:
**Answer:** Temperature is directly proportional to the average translational kinetic energy of the particles in a substance.

**Q3363.** The specific heat of a gas in an isothermal process is:
**Answer:** In an isothermal process (ΔT=0), heat can be added without changing the temperature. Since C = Q/(mΔT), dividing by zero gives an infinite specific heat.

**Q3364.** The specific heat of a gas in an adiabatic process is:
**Answer:** In an adiabatic process, there is no heat exchange (Q=0). Since C = Q/(mΔT), the specific heat is zero.

**Q3365.** The ratio of the molar specific heats, γ = Cₚ/Cᵥ, for a monatomic gas is:
**Answer:** For a monatomic gas, Cᵥ = (3/2)R and Cₚ = (5/2)R, so γ = (5/2)R / (3/2)R = 5/3 ≈ 1.67.

**Q3366.** The ratio of the molar specific heats, γ = Cₚ/Cᵥ, for a diatomic gas is:
**Answer:** For a diatomic gas, Cᵥ = (5/2)R and Cₚ = (7/2)R, so γ = (7/2)R / (5/2)R = 7/5 = 1.4.

**Q3367.** The work done in an isochoric process is:
**Answer:** In an isochoric process, the volume is constant (ΔV=0). Since W = PΔV, no work is done.

**Q3368.** A Carnot cycle consists of:
**Answer:** A Carnot cycle is a theoretical thermodynamic cycle consisting of four reversible steps: isothermal expansion, adiabatic expansion, isothermal compression, and adiabatic compression.

**Q3369.** The efficiency of a Carnot engine is given by:
**Answer:** The efficiency of a Carnot engine depends only on the absolute temperatures of the cold (Tc) and hot (Th) reservoirs.

**Q3370.** The first law of thermodynamics can be written as:
**Answer:** This equation states that the change in a system's internal energy (ΔU) is equal to the heat added to the system (Q) minus the work done by the system (W).

**Q3371.** The change in entropy is defined as:
**Answer:** For a reversible process, the change in entropy (ΔS) is the heat transferred (ΔQ) divided by the absolute temperature (T) at which the transfer occurs.

**Q3372.** The area under a T-S diagram represents:
**Answer:** Since ΔQ = TΔS for a reversible process, the heat transferred is the integral of TdS, which is the area under the curve on a Temperature-Entropy (T-S) diagram.

**Q3373.** A perpetual motion machine of the first kind is impossible because it would violate the:
**Answer:** This type of machine would create energy from nothing, violating the first law (conservation of energy).

**Q3374.** A perpetual motion machine of the second kind is impossible because it would violate the:
**Answer:** This type of machine would convert heat completely into work with 100% efficiency, which violates the second law.

**Q3375.** The Clausius statement of the second law of thermodynamics states that:
**Answer:** This statement describes the natural direction of heat flow and is the principle behind refrigeration.

**Q3376.** The Kelvin-Planck statement of the second law of thermodynamics states that:
**Answer:** This statement means it's impossible to create a device operating in a cycle that extracts heat from a single reservoir and converts it entirely into work.

**Q3377.** The heat capacity of a body is the amount of heat required to raise its temperature by:
**Answer:** This is the definition of heat capacity (an extensive property), which is different from specific heat capacity (an intensive property).

**Q3378.** The process of converting a liquid to a vapor at a temperature below its boiling point is called:
**Answer:** Evaporation is a surface phenomenon where a liquid turns into a gas at any temperature below the boiling point.

**Q3379.** The amount of heat required to change the state of a substance without changing its temperature is called:
**Answer:** Latent heat is the energy absorbed or released during a phase transition (like melting or boiling) that occurs at a constant temperature.

**Q3380.** The latent heat of vaporization is the heat required to:
**Answer:** The term 'vaporization' refers to boiling. It's the energy needed to overcome intermolecular forces in the liquid state.

**Q3381.** The critical temperature of a gas is the temperature:
**Answer:** Above the critical temperature, a substance exists as a supercritical fluid, which has properties of both a gas and a liquid.

**Q3382.** The Joule-Thomson effect describes the change in temperature of a gas when it is:
**Answer:** This process, also known as throttling, is a constant enthalpy process and is fundamental to refrigeration and gas liquefaction.

**Q3383.** For an ideal gas, the Joule-Thomson coefficient is:
**Answer:** An ideal gas has no intermolecular forces, so its temperature does not change upon free expansion (throttling).

**Q3384.** The temperature at which the Joule-Thomson coefficient changes sign is called the:
**Answer:** Below the inversion temperature, a gas cools upon expansion (positive coefficient). Above it, the gas heats up (negative coefficient).

**Q3385.** The mean free path of a gas molecule is the average distance traveled:
**Answer:** This is a key concept in the kinetic theory of gases that relates to properties like viscosity and thermal conductivity.

**Q3386.** The pressure of an ideal gas is proportional to the:
**Answer:** According to the kinetic theory of gases, pressure arises from the collisions of molecules with the container walls.

**Q3387.** The root mean square speed of the molecules of a gas is proportional to:
**Answer:** The root mean square speed is given by v\_rms = √(3RT/M), so it is proportional to √T.

**Q3388.** The Maxwell-Boltzmann distribution describes the:
**Answer:** The Maxwell-Boltzmann distribution is a probability distribution for the speeds of particles in a gas at a certain temperature.

**Q3389.** The law of equipartition of energy states that the average energy associated with each degree of freedom of a molecule is:
**Answer:** This law states that the total energy of a system is shared equally among all its degrees of freedom, where k is the Boltzmann constant.

**Q3390.** A monatomic gas has how many degrees of freedom?
**Answer:** A monatomic gas (like Helium or Neon) has three translational degrees of freedom (movement in x, y, and z directions).

**Q3391.** A diatomic gas has how many degrees of freedom at ordinary temperatures?
**Answer:** A diatomic gas (like O₂ or N₂) has three translational and two rotational degrees of freedom at ordinary temperatures.

**Q3392.** The van der Waals equation of state is a modification of the ideal gas law that takes into account:
**Answer:** The van der Waals equation provides a more realistic model for the behavior of real gases by correcting for molecular size and attraction.

**Q3393.** Stefan's law states that the total energy radiated per unit surface area of a black body per unit time is proportional to the:
**Answer:** This is the Stefan-Boltzmann law, expressed as P = σAT⁴, which describes the power radiated from a black body.

**Q3394.** Wien's displacement law states that the peak wavelength of black-body radiation is:
**Answer:** This law (λ\_max = b/T) explains why hotter objects glow with shorter wavelengths (e.g., from red hot to white hot).

**Q3395.** A black body is an object that:
**Answer:** A black body is an idealized physical body that absorbs all incident electromagnetic radiation, regardless of frequency or angle of incidence.

**Q3396.** The emissivity of a black body is:
**Answer:** Emissivity is a measure of how well a surface radiates energy. A perfect black body is a perfect emitter, so its emissivity is 1.

**Q3397.** The thermal conductivity of a material is a measure of its ability to:
**Answer:** Thermal conductivity quantifies how effectively a material transfers heat through conduction.

**Q3398.** The rate of heat conduction through a material is proportional to the:
**Answer:** This is described by Fourier's law of heat conduction, which states that the rate of heat flow is proportional to the area and the temperature gradient.

**Q3399.** A system absorbs 2000 J of heat from its surroundings and does 500 J of work on its surroundings. What is the change in the internal energy of the system?
**Answer:** According to the First Law of Thermodynamics, ΔU = Q - W. Here, Q is positive (+2000 J) because heat is absorbed, and W is positive (+500 J) because work is done by the system. Therefore, ΔU = 2000 J - 500 J = 1500 J.

**Q3400.** The SI unit of thermal conductivity is:
**Answer:** The unit for thermal conductivity is watts per meter-Kelvin.

**Q3401.** Newton's law of cooling states that the rate of loss of heat from a body is proportional to the:
**Answer:** This law is an approximation that applies when the temperature difference between the object and its surroundings is small.

**Q3402.** The thermodynamic temperature scale is also known as the:
**Answer:** The Kelvin scale is the standard thermodynamic temperature scale, as it is based on an absolute zero point.

**Q3403.** The triple point of water is defined to be exactly:
**Answer:** This fixed point is used to define the Kelvin scale. It is also equivalent to 0.01 °C.

**Q3404.** The efficiency of a heat engine is defined as the ratio of:
**Answer:** Efficiency measures how effectively a device converts the heat it receives into useful work.

**Q3405.** The entropy of the universe is:
**Answer:** According to the second law of thermodynamics, the entropy of the universe (an isolated system) always increases in any spontaneous process.

**Q3406.** Which statement best describes a reversible process?
**Answer:** A reversible process is defined as one that can be reversed by an infinitesimal change in conditions, ensuring the system remains in equilibrium throughout.

**Q3407.** All natural processes are:
**Answer:** All real-world processes are irreversible due to factors like friction, heat loss, and other dissipative effects that increase the total entropy.

**Q3408.** The enthalpy of a system is defined as:
**Answer:** Enthalpy (H) is a thermodynamic property defined as the sum of the internal energy (U) and the product of pressure (P) and volume (V).

**Q3409.** The change in enthalpy is equal to the heat transferred at constant:
**Answer:** This is a key property of enthalpy, making it very useful for analyzing processes that occur at constant pressure, such as many chemical reactions.

**Q3410.** The Gibbs free energy of a system is defined as:
**Answer:** Gibbs free energy (G) is defined as enthalpy (H) minus the product of temperature (T) and entropy (S).

**Q3411.** A spontaneous process occurs at constant temperature and pressure if the change in Gibbs free energy is:
**Answer:** A negative change in Gibbs free energy (ΔG < 0) indicates that a process is spontaneous under these conditions.

**Q3412.** A gas inside a piston expands from an initial volume of 0.2 m³ to a final volume of 0.5 m³ at a constant pressure of 150 kPa. How much work is done by the gas?
**Answer:** For an isobaric (constant pressure) process, work done is W = PΔV. First, convert pressure: 150 kPa = 150,000 Pa. The change in volume is ΔV = 0.5 m³ - 0.2 m³ = 0.3 m³. So, W = 150,000 Pa \* 0.3 m³ = 45,000 J, which is equal to 45 kJ.

**Q3413.** The Helmholtz free energy of a system is defined as:
**Answer:** Helmholtz free energy (A) is defined as internal energy (U) minus the product of temperature (T) and entropy (S). It represents the 'useful' work obtainable from a closed system at constant temperature and volume.

---

### Waves (85 Questions)

**Q3414.** Which of a following is a primary characteristic of a mechanical wave?
 **A.** It can travel through a vacuum.
 **B.** It transports energy and matter.
 **C.** Its speed is constant in all media.
 **D.** It requires a medium. ✓
**Answer:** It requires a medium.

**Q3415.** In a transverse wave, the particles of the medium oscillate in which direction relative to the wave's direction of propagation?
 **A.** Parallel to propagation.
 **B.** In a circular motion.
 **C.** They do not oscillate.
 **D.** Perpendicular to propagation. ✓
**Answer:** Perpendicular to propagation.

**Q3416.** The relationship between wave speed (v), frequency (f), and wavelength (λ) is given by:
 **A.** v = f / λ
 **B.** v = λ / f
 **C.** f = v × λ
 **D.** v = f × λ ✓
**Answer:** v = f × λ

**Q3417.** When two waves meet at a point in a medium simultaneously, the resultant displacement is the vector sum of the individual displacements. This is known as the:
 **A.** Huygens' Principle
 **B.** Doppler Effect
 **C.** Principle of Interference
 **D.** Principle of Superposition ✓
**Answer:** Principle of Superposition

**Q3418.** Constructive interference occurs when two waves meet in phase, meaning their path difference is:
**Answer:** (n + 1/2)λ

**Q3419.** When a wave is reflected from a denser medium, what is the phase change it undergoes?
 **A.** 0 degrees
 **B.** 90 degrees
 **C.** 270 degrees
 **D.** 180 degrees ✓
**Answer:** 180 degrees

**Q3420.** Stationary waves are formed by the superposition of two identical waves traveling:
 **A.** In same direction, phase diff π.
 **B.** In same direction, same phase.
 **C.** Perpendicular to each other.
 **D.** In opposite directions. ✓
**Answer:** In opposite directions.

**Q3421.** In a stationary wave, the points of zero amplitude are called:
 **A.** Antinodes
 **B.** Crests
 **C.** Troughs
 **D.** Nodes ✓
**Answer:** Nodes

**Q3422.** The distance between two consecutive nodes in a stationary wave is:
 **A.** λ
 **B.** λ/4
 **C.** 2λ
 **D.** λ/2 ✓
**Answer:** λ/2

**Q3423.** For a stretched string of length L fixed at both ends, the wavelength of the fundamental mode of vibration (first harmonic) is:
 **A.** L
 **B.** L/2
 **C.** 4L
 **D.** 2L ✓
**Answer:** 2L

**Q3424.** For an air column in a pipe closed at one end, which of the following is true for the resonant modes?
 **A.** All harmonics are present.
 **B.** Only even harmonics are present.
 **C.** No harmonics are present.
 **D.** Only odd harmonics are present. ✓
**Answer:** Only odd harmonics are present.

**Q3425.** When a sound source moves towards a stationary observer, the apparent frequency heard by the observer:
 **A.** Decreases
 **B.** Remains the same
 **C.** Becomes zero
 **D.** Increases ✓
**Answer:** Increases

**Q3426.** Which of the following is an application of the Doppler effect?
 **A.** Tuning a musical instrument.
 **B.** Using sunglasses to reduce glare.
 **C.** Creating an echo.
 **D.** Radar gun for speed measurement. ✓
**Answer:** Radar gun for speed measurement.

**Q3427.** The compressions and rarefactions in a longitudinal wave are analogous to what features of a transverse wave?
 **A.** Amplitude and wavelength
 **B.** Nodes and antinodes
 **C.** Frequency and period
 **D.** Crests and troughs ✓
**Answer:** Crests and troughs

**Q3428.** What happens to the wavelength of a wave when it passes from a denser to a rarer medium?
 **A.** It decreases.
 **B.** It remains unchanged.
 **C.** It becomes zero.
 **D.** It increases. ✓
**Answer:** It increases.

**Q3429.** The energy of a wave is directly proportional to the square of its:
 **A.** Wavelength
 **B.** Frequency
 **C.** Speed
 **D.** Amplitude ✓
**Answer:** Amplitude

**Q3430.** In an open organ pipe (open at both ends), the fundamental frequency corresponds to a standing wave with:
 **A.** Nodes at both ends.
 **B.** Node at one end, antinode at other.
 **C.** Two nodes, three antinodes.
 **D.** Antinodes at both ends. ✓
**Answer:** Antinodes at both ends.

**Q3431.** For a given temperature, the speed of sound is greatest in which state of matter?
 **A.** Liquids
 **B.** Gases
 **C.** Plasma
 **D.** Solids ✓
**Answer:** Solids

**Q3432.** The effect of humidity on the speed of sound in air is that the speed:
 **A.** Decreases
 **B.** Remains unchanged
 **C.** Becomes zero
 **D.** Increases ✓
**Answer:** Increases

**Q3433.** Two waves are said to be coherent if they have:
 **A.** Same amplitude and frequency.
 **B.** Same speed and amplitude.
 **C.** Same wavelength and are in phase.
 **D.** Same frequency, constant phase difference. ✓
**Answer:** Same frequency, constant phase difference.

**Q3434.** The loudness of a sound is most closely related to its:
 **A.** Frequency
 **B.** Wavelength
 **C.** Speed
 **D.** Amplitude ✓
**Answer:** Amplitude

**Q3435.** Which phenomenon demonstrates that light waves are transverse?
 **A.** Interference
 **B.** Diffraction
 **C.** Reflection
 **D.** Polarization ✓
**Answer:** Polarization

**Q3436.** If the tension in a stretched string is quadrupled, its fundamental frequency of vibration becomes:
 **A.** Half
 **B.** Quadrupled
 **C.** Unchanged
 **D.** Doubled ✓
**Answer:** Doubled

**Q3437.** A wave pulse is a disturbance that is:
 **A.** Continuous and periodic.
 **B.** Always transverse.
 **C.** Stationary.
 **D.** Of short duration. ✓
**Answer:** Of short duration.

**Q3438.** The time period of a simple pendulum is T. If its length is quadrupled, the new time period will be:
 **A.** T/2
 **B.** 4T
 **C.** T/4
 **D.** 2T ✓
**Answer:** 2T

**Q3439.** A 'shock wave' or 'sonic boom' is produced when an object travels:
 **A.** Slower than sound.
 **B.** At the speed of sound.
 **C.** In a high-speed circular path.
 **D.** Faster than sound. ✓
**Answer:** Faster than sound.

**Q3440.** The quality or timbre of a musical sound depends on:
 **A.** Fundamental frequency only.
 **B.** Amplitude of the wave.
 **C.** Speed of sound.
 **D.** Harmonics and their intensities. ✓
**Answer:** Harmonics and their intensities.

**Q3441.** The bending of waves as they pass through a narrow opening or around an obstacle is called:
 **A.** Reflection
 **B.** Refraction
 **C.** Interference
 **D.** Diffraction ✓
**Answer:** Diffraction

**Q3442.** Two points on a wave are in the same phase if the distance between them is:
 **A.** λ/2
 **B.** λ/4
 **C.** λ/3
 **D.** nλ ✓
**Answer:** nλ

**Q3443.** The speed of a transverse wave on a stretched string is given by v = √(T/μ). The term μ represents:
 **A.** Mass of the string.
 **B.** Length of the string.
 **C.** Tension in the string.
 **D.** Linear mass density. ✓
**Answer:** Linear mass density.

**Q3444.** If the length of a pipe closed at one end is 0.25 m, what is the wavelength of its fundamental resonant frequency?
 **A.** 0.25 m
 **B.** 0.5 m
 **C.** 2.0 m
 **D.** 1.0 m ✓
**Answer:** 1.0 m

**Q3445.** An observer is moving away from a stationary source of sound. The apparent pitch heard by the observer will be:
 **A.** Higher than actual pitch.
 **B.** Same as actual pitch.
 **C.** Fluctuating.
 **D.** Lower than actual pitch. ✓
**Answer:** Lower than actual pitch.

**Q3446.** Which of the following does NOT change when a wave enters a new medium?
 **A.** Speed
 **B.** Wavelength
 **C.** Direction
 **D.** Frequency ✓
**Answer:** Frequency

**Q3447.** The intensity of a wave from a point source decreases with distance (r) from the source according to which relationship?
 **A.** I ∝ 1/r
 **B.** I ∝ r
 **C.** I ∝ r²
 **D.** I ∝ 1/r² ✓
**Answer:** I ∝ 1/r²

**Q3448.** A string vibrates in 5 segments (loops). This corresponds to which harmonic?
 **A.** 3rd harmonic
 **B.** 4th harmonic
 **C.** 10th harmonic
 **D.** 5th harmonic ✓
**Answer:** 5th harmonic

**Q3449.** What is the phase difference between particles at a node and an adjacent antinode in a stationary wave?
 **A.** 0 degrees
 **B.** 180 degrees
 **C.** 360 degrees
 **D.** 90 degrees ✓
**Answer:** 90 degrees

**Q3450.** The phenomenon of 'resonance' occurs when the frequency of an applied force is:
 **A.** Much higher than natural frequency.
 **B.** Much lower than natural frequency.
 **C.** Half the natural frequency.
 **D.** Equal to natural frequency. ✓
**Answer:** Equal to natural frequency.

**Q3451.** A sound wave has a frequency of 500 Hz and a speed of 340 m/s. What is its wavelength?
 **A.** 1.47 m
 **B.** 170000 m
 **C.** 0.50 m
 **D.** 0.68 m ✓
**Answer:** 0.68 m

**Q3452.** If a wave reflects from a 'free end' or a less dense medium, the reflected wave has a phase change of:
 **A.** 90 degrees
 **B.** 180 degrees
 **C.** 270 degrees
 **D.** 0 degrees ✓
**Answer:** 0 degrees

**Q3453.** In the equation for a progressive wave, y = A sin(kx - ωt), the term 'k' is known as the:
 **A.** Amplitude
 **B.** Angular frequency
 **C.** Phase constant
 **D.** Wave number ✓
**Answer:** Wave number

**Q3454.** The energy in a stationary wave is:
 **A.** Transported continuously.
 **B.** Zero everywhere.
 **C.** Maximum at the nodes.
 **D.** Confined between nodes. ✓
**Answer:** Confined between nodes.

**Q3455.** The second overtone of a closed organ pipe is equivalent to which harmonic?
 **A.** 3rd harmonic
 **B.** 4th harmonic
 **C.** 6th harmonic
 **D.** 5th harmonic ✓
**Answer:** 5th harmonic

**Q3456.** The maximum velocity of a particle in a simple harmonic wave (v\_max = ωA) occurs when the particle is at:
 **A.** The extreme position.
 **B.** Midway between mean and extreme.
 **C.** Any point (velocity is constant).
 **D.** The equilibrium position. ✓
**Answer:** The equilibrium position.

**Q3457.** How does the speed of sound in air change if the absolute temperature is quadrupled?
 **A.** Becomes four times faster.
 **B.** Becomes half as fast.
 **C.** Remains unchanged.
 **D.** Becomes twice as fast. ✓
**Answer:** Becomes twice as fast.

**Q3458.** Two sound waves have frequencies of 400 Hz and 405 Hz. What is the time interval between successive beats?
 **A.** 5 s
 **B.** 805 s
 **C.** 0.0025 s
 **D.** 0.2 s ✓
**Answer:** 0.2 s

**Q3459.** What is the phase difference between two points on a progressive wave separated by a distance of λ/4?
 **A.** π radians
 **B.** 2π radians
 **C.** π/4 radians
 **D.** π/2 radians ✓
**Answer:** π/2 radians

**Q3460.** Red shift in the light from distant galaxies is an example of:
 **A.** Interference
 **B.** Polarization
 **C.** Diffraction
 **D.** The Doppler effect ✓
**Answer:** The Doppler effect

**Q3461.** When a longitudinal wave travels through a medium, what is transported?
 **A.** Matter only
 **B.** Energy and matter
 **C.** Neither energy nor matter
 **D.** Energy only ✓
**Answer:** Energy only

**Q3462.** The second overtone of a vibrating string fixed at both ends corresponds to which harmonic?
 **A.** 2nd harmonic
 **B.** 4th harmonic
 **C.** 1st harmonic
 **D.** 3rd harmonic ✓
**Answer:** 3rd harmonic

**Q3463.** In a displacement-position graph of a wave, the amplitude is represented by the:
 **A.** Horizontal distance between crests.
 **B.** Slope of the graph.
 **C.** Area under one cycle.
 **D.** Maximum vertical displacement. ✓
**Answer:** Maximum vertical displacement.

**Q3464.** In a resonance tube, if the first resonance is at length L₁ and the second is at L₂, the wavelength is:
 **A.** L₂ - L₁
 **B.** (L₂ - L₁)/2
 **C.** L₁ + L₂
 **D.** 2(L₂ - L₁) ✓
**Answer:** 2(L₂ - L₁)

**Q3465.** In the equation y = A sin(kx - ωt), the term ω represents the:
 **A.** Wave number
 **B.** Wave speed
 **C.** Wavelength
 **D.** Angular frequency ✓
**Answer:** Angular frequency

**Q3466.** If the density of a gas is quadrupled at constant pressure, the speed of sound will:
 **A.** Be doubled
 **B.** Become four times
 **C.** Remain unchanged
 **D.** Be halved ✓
**Answer:** Be halved

**Q3467.** Destructive interference will be complete if the two interfering waves have:
 **A.** Same freq, different amplitudes, π phase difference.
 **B.** Different freqs, same amplitude, in phase.
 **C.** Same freq, amplitude, and in phase.
 **D.** Same freq, amplitude, and π phase difference. ✓
**Answer:** Same freq, amplitude, and π phase difference.

**Q3468.** All particles in a stationary wave between two consecutive nodes vibrate:
 **A.** With same frequency and amplitude.
 **B.** With different frequencies and amplitudes.
 **C.** In opposite phase, different amplitudes.
 **D.** With same frequency, different amplitudes. ✓
**Answer:** With same frequency, different amplitudes.

**Q3469.** The speed of light in a vacuum is 3×10⁸ m/s. What is its speed in a medium with a refractive index of 1.5?
 **A.** Remains the same.
 **B.** Increases.
 **C.** Becomes 4.5×10⁸ m/s.
 **D.** Becomes 2×10⁸ m/s. ✓
**Answer:** Becomes 2×10⁸ m/s.

**Q3470.** A string of length L has frequency f. If length is halved and tension is doubled, the new frequency will be:
 **A.** f
 **B.** 2f
 **C.** f/√2
 **D.** 2√2 f ✓
**Answer:** 2√2 f

**Q3471.** The 'pitch' of a musical note is determined by its:
 **A.** Amplitude
 **B.** Waveform
 **C.** Speed
 **D.** Frequency ✓
**Answer:** Frequency

**Q3472.** Which of these is NOT a mechanical wave?
 **A.** Sound waves
 **B.** Water waves
 **C.** Waves on a string
 **D.** Radio waves ✓
**Answer:** Radio waves

**Q3473.** The SI unit of wave number (k) is:
 **A.** m
 **B.** s⁻¹
 **C.** rad/s
 **D.** rad/m or m⁻¹ ✓
**Answer:** rad/m or m⁻¹

**Q3474.** At the crest of a transverse wave, the instantaneous acceleration of a particle is:
 **A.** Zero.
 **B.** Maximal and upward.
 **C.** Constant.
 **D.** Maximal and downward. ✓
**Answer:** Maximal and downward.

**Q3475.** The wave velocity (v) is related to the maximum particle velocity (v\_max) by:
 **A.** v = v\_max
 **B.** v = (k/ω)v\_max
 **C.** No general relation.
 **D.** No simple relation. ✓
**Answer:** No simple relation.

**Q3476.** Huygens' principle can be used to explain:
 **A.** Particle nature of light.
 **B.** Photoelectric effect.
 **C.** Quantization of energy.
 **D.** Reflection, refraction, and diffraction. ✓
**Answer:** Reflection, refraction, and diffraction.

**Q3477.** A wave is described by y(x,t) = 0.05 sin(2πx - 4πt). What is its speed?
 **A.** 0.5 m/s
 **B.** 4 m/s
 **C.** 8 m/s
 **D.** 2 m/s ✓
**Answer:** 2 m/s

**Q3478.** Ultrasound has a frequency that is:
 **A.** Below 20 Hz
 **B.** Between 20 Hz and 20 kHz
 **C.** Exactly 20 kHz
 **D.** Above 20 kHz ✓
**Answer:** Above 20 kHz

**Q3479.** When temperature increases, the frequency of a tuning fork:
 **A.** Increases
 **B.** Remains the same
 **C.** Becomes zero
 **D.** Decreases ✓
**Answer:** Decreases

**Q3480.** In stationary waves, the net transfer of energy is:
 **A.** Maximum
 **B.** Constant but not zero
 **C.** Dependent on amplitude
 **D.** Zero ✓
**Answer:** Zero

**Q3481.** A sonometer wire of length L vibrates in its fundamental mode. The standing wave has:
 **A.** One node, one antinode.
 **B.** One node, two antinodes.
 **C.** Two nodes, two antinodes.
 **D.** Two nodes, one antinode. ✓
**Answer:** Two nodes, one antinode.

**Q3482.** Which of the following properties of a wave is independent of the others?
 **A.** Speed
 **B.** Wavelength
 **C.** Frequency
 **D.** Amplitude ✓
**Answer:** Amplitude

**Q3483.** Earthquake waves are an example of:
 **A.** Transverse waves only
 **B.** Longitudinal waves only
 **C.** Electromagnetic waves
 **D.** Both transverse and longitudinal ✓
**Answer:** Both transverse and longitudinal

**Q3484.** Two waves of equal amplitude and frequency interfere. The resultant amplitude is maximum when the phase difference is:
 **A.** π/2
 **B.** π
 **C.** 3π/2
 **D.** 2nπ (n is integer) ✓
**Answer:** 2nπ (n is integer)

**Q3485.** A wave pulse on a string moves from a less dense to a more dense section. The pulse will be:
 **A.** Only transmitted.
 **B.** Only reflected and inverted.
 **C.** Partly transmitted, partly reflected, not inverted.
 **D.** Partly transmitted, partly reflected & inverted. ✓
**Answer:** Partly transmitted, partly reflected & inverted.

**Q3486.** The effect of loading a tuning fork with wax is to:
 **A.** Increase its frequency.
 **B.** Increase amplitude.
 **C.** Decrease amplitude.
 **D.** Decrease its frequency. ✓
**Answer:** Decrease its frequency.

**Q3487.** Sound waves cannot be polarized because they:
 **A.** Are transverse waves.
 **B.** Have a very high speed.
 **C.** Have a very long wavelength.
 **D.** Are longitudinal waves. ✓
**Answer:** Are longitudinal waves.

**Q3488.** Silence zones are created in an auditorium due to:
 **A.** Constructive interference
 **B.** Beats
 **C.** Resonance
 **D.** Destructive interference ✓
**Answer:** Destructive interference

**Q3489.** A simple pendulum has a time period T on Earth. If it is taken to the Moon (gravity is 1/6th of Earth's), its new time period will be:
 **A.** T/6
 **B.** T/√6
 **C.** 6T
 **D.** √6 T ✓
**Answer:** √6 T

**Q3490.** Which statement is true for both longitudinal and transverse waves?
 **A.** They can be polarized.
 **B.** They travel as compressions.
 **C.** Particles vibrate parallel to wave propagation.
 **D.** They transfer energy. ✓
**Answer:** They transfer energy.

**Q3491.** The intensity of a sound wave is proportional to the square of:
 **A.** Wavelength
 **B.** Wave speed
 **C.** Time period
 **D.** Amplitude ✓
**Answer:** Amplitude

**Q3492.** The phenomenon where a vibrating body causes another body to vibrate with the same frequency is called:
 **A.** Beats
 **B.** Resonance
 **C.** Damped oscillation
 **D.** Forced vibration ✓
**Answer:** Forced vibration

**Q3493.** Two cars move in opposite directions at the same speed. If one blows a horn, the pitch heard by the other driver will be:
 **A.** Highest when moving away.
 **B.** Highest when just crossing.
 **C.** Same as the original pitch.
 **D.** Highest when approaching. ✓
**Answer:** Highest when approaching.

**Q3494.** The distance between a node and the next antinode in a stationary wave is:
 **A.** λ
 **B.** λ/2
 **C.** 2λ
 **D.** λ/4 ✓
**Answer:** λ/4

**Q3495.** The spreading of a wave passing through an aperture is more pronounced when the:
 **A.** Wavelength is smaller than aperture.
 **B.** Frequency is very high.
 **C.** Amplitude is large.
 **D.** Wavelength is comparable to aperture. ✓
**Answer:** Wavelength is comparable to aperture.

**Q3496.** A progressive wave is represented by y = 5 sin(100πt - 2πx). What is the wavelength of the wave?
 **A.** 2 m
 **B.** 0.5 m
 **C.** 100 m
 **D.** 1 m ✓
**Answer:** 1 m

**Q3497.** Which of the following is an essential condition for producing beats?
 **A.** The sources have the same frequency.
 **B.** The sources have the same amplitude.
 **C.** The sources are moving.
 **D.** The sources have slightly different frequencies. ✓
**Answer:** The sources have slightly different frequencies.

**Q3498.** Water waves are an example of:
 **A.** Transverse waves only
 **B.** Longitudinal waves only
 **C.** Stationary waves
 **D.** Both transverse and longitudinal waves ✓
**Answer:** Both transverse and longitudinal waves
