### Work And Energy (96 Questions)

**Q3499.** Work done is a product of force and displacement. Which type of product is it?
 **A.** Vector (Cross) Product
 **B.** Simple Algebraic Product
 **C.** Tensor Product
 **D.** Scalar (Dot) Product ✓
**Answer:** Scalar (Dot) Product

**Q3500.** The work done will be negative when the angle between force and displacement is:
 **A.** Between 90° and 180° ✓
**Answer:** Between 90° and 180°

**Q3501.** A force of (3i + 4j) N displaces a body by (3i + 4j) m. The work done is:
 **A.** 12 J
 **B.** 7 J
 **C.** 0 J
 **D.** 25 J ✓
**Answer:** 25 J

**Q3502.** Which of the following is the unit of work in the British Engineering system?
 **A.** Foot-pound (ft-lb) ✓
**Answer:** Foot-pound (ft-lb)

**Q3503.** The area under a Force-Displacement graph represents:
 **A.** Change in Momentum
 **B.** Work Done ✓
**Answer:** Work Done

**Q3504.** If the velocity of a moving object is doubled, its kinetic energy becomes:
 **A.** Four times ✓
**Answer:** Four times

**Q3505.** The work-energy principle states that the net work done on a body equals the change in its:
 **A.** Potential Energy
 **B.** Total Energy
 **C.** Kinetic Energy ✓
**Answer:** Kinetic Energy

**Q3506.** Gravitational Potential Energy of a body is independent of:
 **A.** Its mass
 **B.** Its height from the reference point
 **C.** The value of 'g'
 **D.** The path followed to reach that height ✓
**Answer:** The path followed to reach that height

**Q3507.** A 2 kg mass falls from a height of 5 m. The loss in its potential energy is (g=9.8 m/s²):
 **A.** 10 J
 **B.** 49 J
 **C.** 19.6 J
 **D.** 98 J ✓
**Answer:** 98 J

**Q3508.** Which of the following is a non-conservative force?
 **A.** Gravitational Force
 **B.** Elastic Spring Force
 **C.** Electrostatic Force
 **D.** Air Resistance ✓
**Answer:** Air Resistance

**Q3509.** The law of conservation of energy states that in an isolated system, the total energy:
 **A.** Always increases
 **B.** Always decreases
 **C.** Is always zero
 **D.** Remains constant ✓
**Answer:** Remains constant

**Q3510.** The rate at which work is done is known as:
 **A.** Impulse
 **B.** Power ✓
**Answer:** Power

**Q3511.** One horsepower (hp) is equal to approximately:
 **A.** 1000 Watts
 **B.** 550 Watts
 **C.** 1 Watt
 **D.** 746 Watts ✓
**Answer:** 746 Watts

**Q3512.** Power can also be expressed as the dot product of:
 **A.** Force and displacement
 **B.** Force and acceleration
 **C.** Force and time
 **D.** Force and velocity ✓
**Answer:** Force and velocity

**Q3513.** Escape velocity is the minimum velocity required for an object to:
 **A.** Orbit the Earth
 **B.** Reach the Moon
 **C.** Stay stationary in space
 **D.** Overcome the Earth's gravitational pull ✓
**Answer:** Overcome the Earth's gravitational pull

**Q3514.** The value of escape velocity from the Earth's surface is approximately:
 **A.** 7.9 km/s
 **B.** 9.8 m/s
 **C.** 3.0 x 10⁸ m/s
 **D.** 11.2 km/s ✓
**Answer:** 11.2 km/s

**Q3515.** The work done in holding a 25 kg bag while waiting for a bus is:
 **A.** 25 J
 **B.** 245 J
 **C.** Infinite
 **D.** Zero ✓
**Answer:** Zero

**Q3516.** An electron-volt (eV) is a unit of:
 **A.** Voltage
 **B.** Power
 **C.** Charge
 **D.** Energy ✓
**Answer:** Energy

**Q3517.** The kinetic energy of a 4 kg mass moving at 3 m/s is:
 **A.** 12 J
 **B.** 6 J
 **C.** 36 J
 **D.** 18 J ✓
**Answer:** 18 J

**Q3518.** A spring is compressed by a distance 'x'. The elastic potential energy stored in it is:
 **A.** kx
 **B.** ½kx
 **C.** kx²
 **D.** ½kx² ✓
**Answer:** ½kx²

**Q3519.** The escape velocity of an object is independent of its:
 **A.** Mass of the planet
 **B.** Radius of the planet
 **C.** Gravitational constant
 **D.** Mass of the object ✓
**Answer:** Mass of the object

**Q3520.** What happens to the total energy of a simple pendulum as it oscillates?
 **A.** It increases
 **B.** It decreases due to friction
 **C.** It is maximum at the mean position
 **D.** It remains constant if there is no air resistance ✓
**Answer:** It remains constant if there is no air resistance

**Q3521.** A motor rated at 2238 W is equivalent to a motor of:
 **A.** 1 hp
 **B.** 2 hp
 **C.** 4 hp
 **D.** 3 hp ✓
**Answer:** 3 hp

**Q3522.** If a body's momentum is doubled, its kinetic energy will be:
 **A.** Doubled
 **B.** Halved
 **C.** Unchanged
 **D.** Four times greater ✓
**Answer:** Four times greater

**Q3523.** Work done by the force of friction is always:
 **A.** Positive
 **B.** Zero
 **C.** Either positive or negative
 **D.** Negative ✓
**Answer:** Negative

**Q3524.** A light and a heavy body have equal kinetic energy. Which has greater momentum?
 **A.** The light body
 **B.** Both have equal momentum
 **C.** Cannot be determined
 **D.** The heavy body ✓
**Answer:** The heavy body

**Q3525.** Which of the following is not a unit of energy?
 **A.** Joule
 **B.** Calorie
 **C.** Kilowatt-hour
 **D.** Kilowatt ✓
**Answer:** Kilowatt

**Q3526.** When a conservative force does positive work on a body, the potential energy associated with that force:
 **A.** Increases
 **B.** Remains constant
 **C.** Becomes zero
 **D.** Decreases ✓
**Answer:** Decreases

**Q3527.** A car's engine provides a force of 500 N to maintain a constant speed of 20 m/s. What is the power of the engine?
 **A.** 25 W
 **B.** 520 W
 **C.** 1000 W
 **D.** 10,000 W ✓
**Answer:** 10,000 W

**Q3528.** A ball is dropped from height h. Just before hitting the ground, its energy is:
 **A.** Purely potential
 **B.** Partly kinetic and partly potential
 **C.** Zero
 **D.** Purely kinetic ✓
**Answer:** Purely kinetic

**Q3529.** Which statement is true for the work done by a non-conservative force?
 **A.** It is always zero in a closed path.
 **B.** It is path-independent.
 **C.** It is always a positive value.
 **D.** It can change the total mechanical energy of a system. ✓
**Answer:** It can change the total mechanical energy of a system.

**Q3530.** If the spring constant 'k' of a spring is doubled, the energy it stores for the same compression 'x' will be:
 **A.** Halved
 **B.** Four times
 **C.** Unchanged
 **D.** Doubled ✓
**Answer:** Doubled

**Q3531.** A 1000 kg car and a 2000 kg truck have the same kinetic energy. Which is moving faster?
 **A.** The truck
 **B.** They have the same speed
 **C.** Cannot be determined
 **D.** The car ✓
**Answer:** The car

**Q3532.** An object in a satellite orbiting the Earth experiences 'weightlessness' because:
 **A.** It is too far from Earth for gravity to act.
 **B.** The satellite shields it from gravity.
 **C.** The centrifugal force perfectly balances gravity.
 **D.** It is in a continuous state of free fall around the Earth. ✓
**Answer:** It is in a continuous state of free fall around the Earth.

**Q3533.** The orbital velocity for a satellite close to the Earth's surface is approximately:
 **A.** 11.2 km/s
 **B.** 9.8 km/s
 **C.** 1.4 km/s
 **D.** 7.9 km/s ✓
**Answer:** 7.9 km/s

**Q3534.** If a force F is applied to a body and it moves with velocity v, the instantaneous power is:
 **A.** F/v
 **B.** Fv²
 **C.** F/v²
 **D.** Fv ✓
**Answer:** Fv

**Q3535.** The source of the Sun's energy is:
 **A.** Chemical reactions
 **B.** Nuclear fission
 **C.** Burning of hydrogen gas
 **D.** Nuclear fusion ✓
**Answer:** Nuclear fusion

**Q3536.** A pump lifts 200 kg of water to a height of 20 m in 10 seconds. The power of the pump is (g=10 m/s²):
 **A.** 400 W
 **B.** 2000 W
 **C.** 200 W
 **D.** 4000 W ✓
**Answer:** 4000 W

**Q3537.** Which of these energy transformations occurs in a solar cell?
 **A.** Heat energy to electrical energy
 **B.** Light energy to chemical energy
 **C.** Chemical energy to electrical energy
 **D.** Light energy to electrical energy ✓
**Answer:** Light energy to electrical energy

**Q3538.** If a force is applied but the object does not move, the work done is:
 **A.** Maximum
 **B.** Minimum
 **C.** Negative
 **D.** Zero ✓
**Answer:** Zero

**Q3539.** A cyclist comes to a skidding stop in 10 m. During this process, the force on the cycle due to the road is 200 N and is directly opposed to the motion. The work done by the road on the cycle is:
 **A.** 2000 J
 **B.** Zero
 **C.** 20 J
 **D.** -2000 J ✓
**Answer:** -2000 J

**Q3540.** Einstein's mass-energy equation is:
 **A.** E = mc
 **B.** E = m/c²
 **C.** E = (mc)²
 **D.** E = mc² ✓
**Answer:** E = mc²

**Q3541.** The total energy of a satellite orbiting the Earth is:
 **A.** Positive
 **B.** Zero
 **C.** Infinite
 **D.** Negative ✓
**Answer:** Negative

**Q3542.** If the radius of a planet is halved while its mass remains the same, the escape velocity will:
 **A.** Be halved
 **B.** Be doubled
 **C.** Remain the same
 **D.** Increase by a factor of √2 ✓
**Answer:** Increase by a factor of √2

**Q3543.** Work done by a variable force can be found by dividing the:
 **A.** Force vs Time graph into small intervals
 **B.** Displacement vs Time graph into small intervals
 **C.** Velocity vs Time graph into small intervals
 **D.** Force vs Displacement graph into small intervals ✓
**Answer:** Force vs Displacement graph into small intervals

**Q3544.** A man pushes a wall and fails to displace it. He does:
 **A.** Positive work
 **B.** Negative work
 **C.** Maximum work
 **D.** No work at all ✓
**Answer:** No work at all

**Q3545.** A body is falling freely under gravity. Its:
 **A.** Potential energy increases
 **B.** Kinetic energy decreases
 **C.** Total momentum is conserved
 **D.** Total mechanical energy is conserved ✓
**Answer:** Total mechanical energy is conserved

**Q3546.** Two objects with masses m₁ and m₂ have the same kinetic energy. The ratio of their speeds (v₁/v₂) is:
 **A.** m₁/m₂
 **B.** m₂/m₁
 **C.** √(m₁/m₂)
 **D.** √(m₂/m₁) ✓
**Answer:** √(m₂/m₁)

**Q3547.** When you stretch a rubber band, you are storing:
 **A.** Kinetic energy
 **B.** Gravitational potential energy
 **C.** Chemical energy
 **D.** Elastic potential energy ✓
**Answer:** Elastic potential energy

**Q3548.** Angular displacement is typically measured in which unit in the SI system?
 **A.** Meters (m)
 **B.** Hertz (Hz)
 **C.** Degrees (°)
 **D.** Radians (rad) ✓
**Answer:** Radians (rad)

**Q3549.** One complete revolution is equal to how many radians?
 **A.** π radians
 **B.** π/2 radians
 **C.** 4π radians
 **D.** 2π radians ✓
**Answer:** 2π radians

**Q3550.** The rate of change of angular displacement is known as:
 **A.** Angular acceleration
 **B.** Linear velocity
 **C.** Angular momentum
 **D.** Angular velocity ✓
**Answer:** Angular velocity

**Q3551.** What is the relationship between linear velocity (v) and angular velocity (ω) for an object in circular motion of radius r?
 **A.** v = ω/r
 **B.** v = r/ω
 **C.** v = r²ω
 **D.** v = rω ✓
**Answer:** v = rω

**Q3552.** The direction of angular velocity is determined by the:
 **A.** Left-hand rule
 **B.** Direction of tangential velocity
 **C.** Direction of centripetal force
 **D.** Right-hand rule ✓
**Answer:** Right-hand rule

**Q3553.** The acceleration directed towards the center of a circular path is called:
 **A.** Tangential acceleration
 **B.** Angular acceleration
 **C.** Linear acceleration
 **D.** Centripetal acceleration ✓
**Answer:** Centripetal acceleration

**Q3554.** The formula for centripetal acceleration (a\_c) is:
 **A.** rω
 **B.** r²ω
 **C.** v/r
 **D.** v²/r ✓
**Answer:** v²/r

**Q3555.** The force responsible for keeping an object in a circular path is known as:
 **A.** Centrifugal force
 **B.** Gravitational force
 **C.** Frictional force
 **D.** Centripetal force ✓
**Answer:** Centripetal force

**Q3556.** According to Newton's second law, the formula for centripetal force (F\_c) is:
 **A.** mvr
 **B.** mv/r
 **C.** mr²/v
 **D.** mv²/r ✓
**Answer:** mv²/r

**Q3557.** The rotational analogue of mass is:
 **A.** Torque
 **B.** Angular momentum
 **C.** Angular velocity
 **D.** Moment of inertia ✓
**Answer:** Moment of inertia

**Q3558.** The moment of inertia of an object depends on:
 **A.** Its angular velocity
 **B.** Its angular acceleration
 **C.** The applied torque
 **D.** Its mass and the distribution of mass about the axis of rotation ✓
**Answer:** Its mass and the distribution of mass about the axis of rotation

**Q3559.** The rotational analogue of force is:
 **A.** Inertia
 **B.** Momentum
 **C.** Power
 **D.** Torque ✓
**Answer:** Torque

**Q3560.** The rotational form of Newton's second law is:
 **A.** α = τ/I²
 **B.** I = τα
 **C.** τ = Iω
 **D.** τ = Iα ✓
**Answer:** τ = Iα

**Q3561.** The rotational analogue of linear momentum (p = mv) is:
 **A.** Moment of inertia (I)
 **B.** Angular velocity (ω)
 **C.** Torque (τ)
 **D.** Angular momentum (L = Iω) ✓
**Answer:** Angular momentum (L = Iω)

**Q3562.** The law of conservation of angular momentum states that if no external torque acts on a system, its total angular momentum:
 **A.** Increases
 **B.** Decreases
 **C.** Becomes zero
 **D.** Remains constant ✓
**Answer:** Remains constant

**Q3563.** A diver pulls her arms and legs in while spinning. Her angular velocity:
 **A.** Decreases
 **B.** Remains the same
 **C.** Becomes zero
 **D.** Increases ✓
**Answer:** Increases

**Q3564.** The formula for rotational kinetic energy is:
 **A.** ½Iω
 **B.** ½I²ω
 **C.** Iω²
 **D.** ½Iω² ✓
**Answer:** ½Iω²

**Q3565.** An object rolling down an incline without slipping has:
 **A.** Only rotational kinetic energy
 **B.** Only translational kinetic energy
 **C.** Only potential energy
 **D.** Both translational and rotational kinetic energy ✓
**Answer:** Both translational and rotational kinetic energy

**Q3566.** Which object will reach the bottom of an incline first if released from rest, a solid sphere or a hollow sphere of the same mass and radius?
 **A.** The hollow sphere
 **B.** They will reach at the same time
 **C.** It depends on their mass
 **D.** The solid sphere ✓
**Answer:** The solid sphere

**Q3567.** The critical velocity of a satellite in a low Earth orbit is also known as:
 **A.** Escape velocity
 **B.** Terminal velocity
 **C.** Angular velocity
 **D.** Orbital velocity ✓
**Answer:** Orbital velocity

**Q3568.** In a geostationary orbit, a satellite's orbital period is:
 **A.** 12 hours
 **B.** 90 minutes
 **C.** 365 days
 **D.** 24 hours ✓
**Answer:** 24 hours

**Q3569.** The sensation of weightlessness in a satellite is due to:
 **A.** The absence of gravity
 **B.** The balancing of gravitational and centrifugal forces
 **C.** The high speed of the satellite
 **D.** The satellite being in a constant state of free-fall ✓
**Answer:** The satellite being in a constant state of free-fall

**Q3570.** The concept of 'artificial gravity' in a space station can be created by:
 **A.** Increasing the station's mass
 **B.** Moving to a lower orbit
 **C.** Using powerful magnets
 **D.** Spinning the space station ✓
**Answer:** Spinning the space station

**Q3571.** The frequency of rotation for a space station to simulate Earth's gravity (g) depends on its:
 **A.** Mass
 **B.** Color
 **C.** Altitude
 **D.** Radius ✓
**Answer:** Radius

**Q3572.** What happens to the weight of a person in a lift accelerating downwards?
 **A.** It increases
 **B.** It remains the same
 **C.** It becomes zero
 **D.** It decreases ✓
**Answer:** It decreases

**Q3573.** A car is moving on a banked road. The necessary centripetal force is provided by:
 **A.** Friction only
 **B.** The vertical component of the normal force
 **C.** The weight of the car
 **D.** The horizontal component of the normal force ✓
**Answer:** The horizontal component of the normal force

**Q3574.** A flywheel is used in machines to:
 **A.** Increase the speed
 **B.** Decrease the speed
 **C.** Reduce the machine's weight
 **D.** Store rotational energy and smooth out motion ✓
**Answer:** Store rotational energy and smooth out motion

**Q3575.** The unit of angular acceleration is:
 **A.** rad/s
 **B.** rev/s
 **C.** m/s²
 **D.** rad/s² ✓
**Answer:** rad/s²

**Q3576.** If the angular velocity of an object changes from 2 rad/s to 10 rad/s in 4 seconds, its angular acceleration is:
 **A.** 8 rad/s²
 **B.** 4 rad/s²
 **C.** 3 rad/s²
 **D.** 2 rad/s² ✓
**Answer:** 2 rad/s²

**Q3577.** Tangential acceleration in circular motion is responsible for:
 **A.** Changing the direction of motion
 **B.** Keeping the object in a circle
 **C.** Both A and C
 **D.** Changing the speed of motion ✓
**Answer:** Changing the speed of motion

**Q3578.** A body in uniform circular motion has:
 **A.** Constant velocity
 **B.** Constant acceleration
 **C.** Zero acceleration
 **D.** Constant speed ✓
**Answer:** Constant speed

**Q3579.** The moment of inertia of a thin hoop of mass M and radius R about its center is:
 **A.** ½MR²
 **B.** ⅖MR²
 **C.** ⅔MR²
 **D.** MR² ✓
**Answer:** MR²

**Q3580.** The term 'centrifugal force' is often described as a:
 **A.** Real force directed inwards
 **B.** Real force directed outwards
 **C.** Gravitational force
 **D.** Fictitious or pseudo force ✓
**Answer:** Fictitious or pseudo force

**Q3581.** Which of the following has the largest moment of inertia for the same mass M and radius R?
 **A.** A solid sphere
 **B.** A solid disk
 **C.** A solid cylinder
 **D.** A thin hoop ✓
**Answer:** A thin hoop

**Q3582.** The time period of a geostationary satellite is:
 **A.** Equal to the time period of the Sun
 **B.** Equal to the time period of the Moon
 **C.** 90 minutes
 **D.** Equal to the rotational period of the Earth ✓
**Answer:** Equal to the rotational period of the Earth

**Q3583.** If a car makes a turn on a flat road, the centripetal force is provided by:
 **A.** The car's engine
 **B.** The normal force
 **C.** The weight of the car
 **D.** The force of friction between the tires and the road ✓
**Answer:** The force of friction between the tires and the road

**Q3584.** The angular momentum of a particle moving in a straight line is:
 **A.** Always zero
 **B.** Always infinite
 **C.** Constant
 **D.** Zero only if the line passes through the origin ✓
**Answer:** Zero only if the line passes through the origin

**Q3585.** The units of angular momentum are:
 **A.** kg m/s
 **B.** kg m/s²
 **C.** kg m²/s²
 **D.** kg m²/s ✓
**Answer:** kg m²/s

**Q3586.** A solid cylinder and a hollow cylinder have the same mass and radius. Which has a greater moment of inertia?
 **A.** The solid cylinder
 **B.** They have the same moment of inertia
 **C.** It depends on their length
 **D.** The hollow cylinder ✓
**Answer:** The hollow cylinder

**Q3587.** If the Earth were to shrink to half its present radius with its mass remaining constant, the length of the day would:
 **A.** Increase
 **B.** Remain the same
 **C.** Become zero
 **D.** Decrease ✓
**Answer:** Decrease

**Q3588.** A body is moving in a circle at a constant speed. Which statement is TRUE?
 **A.** There is no force acting on the body.
 **B.** There is no acceleration.
 **C.** Work is being done on the body.
 **D.** The velocity is changing. ✓
**Answer:** The velocity is changing.

**Q3589.** The SI unit of torque is:
 **A.** Newton (N)
 **B.** Joule (J)
 **C.** Watt (W)
 **D.** Newton-meter (N·m) ✓
**Answer:** Newton-meter (N·m)

**Q3590.** A satellite in a circular orbit around the Earth is an example where:
 **A.** Linear momentum is conserved.
 **B.** Kinetic energy is conserved.
 **C.** Potential energy is conserved.
 **D.** Angular momentum is conserved. ✓
**Answer:** Angular momentum is conserved.

**Q3591.** The total kinetic energy of a rolling hoop is:
 **A.** Equal to its translational KE
 **B.** Equal to its rotational KE
 **C.** Zero
 **D.** The sum of its translational and rotational KE ✓
**Answer:** The sum of its translational and rotational KE

**Q3592.** For a point mass M at a distance R from the axis of rotation, the moment of inertia is:
 **A.** ½MR²
 **B.** ⅖MR²
 **C.** MR² ✓
**Answer:** MR²

**Q3593.** If an object's moment of inertia is large, it is difficult to:
 **A.** Start it rotating
 **B.** Stop it from rotating
 **C.** Change its angular velocity
 **D.** All of the above ✓
**Answer:** All of the above

**Q3594.** The height of a geostationary satellite above the Earth's equator is approximately:
 **A.** 3600 km
 **B.** 360,000 km
 **C.** 3,600,000 km
 **D.** 36,000 km ✓
**Answer:** 36,000 km
